# Pi sweep review — CastCircle

Exhaustive per-file pass: 160 code files across 82 batches — model ds4/GLM-5.3-Flash-Q2:off — 2026-08-30.

## Findings

- [medium] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:130-142 — `_step` treats any error message containing 'already exists' as benign already-applied and returns without rethrowing; a real failure whose message merely mentions "already exists" (e.g. a UNIQUE constraint or table-exists error from a different cause) is swallowed, so the migration silently reports success and the missing column/index is never repaired — later queries die with "no such column" forever, exactly the failure mode the docstring warns about — narrow the benign check to the specific duplicate-column/already-applied error text Drift emits and rethrow everything else (the current `duplicate column name` branch is fine; only the `already exists` substring is over-broad).
- [medium] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:146-188 — `onUpgrade` uses `if (from < N)` guards instead of a completed-step ledger, so a partial upgrade that throws mid-way (e.g. after the v4 column but before the v5 columns) leaves `user_version` unadvanced and re-runs the earlier steps on the next launch; combined with the over-broad `_step` swallow this can advance `user_version` past a step that actually failed, permanently masking the missing column — record per-step completion (e.g. a `schema_migrations` table) or at minimum let `_step` rethrow on non-benign errors so the version is not advanced past a failed step.
- [low] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:316-334 — `_openConnection` sets `PRAGMA synchronous=NORMAL` under WAL, which can lose the last few transactions on an OS crash; the comment acknowledges this is acceptable for re-syncable local state, but recordings' `remoteUrl` upload markers and cast `joinedAt` are also stored here and are NOT re-syncable from anywhere — if the durability trade-off is intentional, gate it behind a flag or document which tables are re-syncable; otherwise use `synchronous=FULL` for the recording/cast tables.
- [low] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:119 — `AppDatabase.forTesting(super.e)` is declared but never used in this file; if the generated `_$AppDatabase` does not define that constructor this is a compile error, and if it does, the testing constructor bypasses `_openConnection`'s WAL/busy_timeout setup, so tests exercise a differently-configured DB than production — verify the generated part file declares it, or drop it.
- [low] lib/data/models/cast_member_model.dart:10-13 — `CastRole.fromString` silently degrades unknown role strings to `CastRole.primary`; a malformed or newer-version row (e.g. a genuine 'organizer' typo'd to 'orgnaizer') is promoted to primary, which in this app is the role that can invite/manage cast — the permissive fallback should resolve to the least-privileged role (`understudy`) or surface an error, not the most-privileged one.
- [low] lib/data/models/cast_member_model.dart:46 — `hasJoined` derives join state solely from `userId != null`; `joinedAt` is ignored, so a member with `joinedAt` set but `userId` null (or vice versa after a partial sync) reports the wrong join state to the UI — derive from both fields or document the invariant that they are set together.
- [info] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:10 — `part 'app_database.g.dart'` is generated code not shown; verify it is not committed with embedded credentials (Supabase/Drift generated files occasionally inline keys) — verify it is not committed.
- [info] lib/data/models/cast_member_model.dart:71 lines — no secrets or credentials present in this file — verify it is not committed (no secret-looking content found; routine check).

## Coverage
lib/data/database/app_database.dart — findings: 5
lib/data/models/cast_member_model.dart — findings: 2
- [info] lib/data/models/production_models.dart:19 — `locale` silently defaults to 'en-US' when omitted, and `copyWith` cannot clear `joinCode`/`scriptPath` (null means "keep"), so a production whose locale/join code is unknown resolves to the permissive/first value rather than an explicit error — consequence: STT locale and join-code state can be wrong with no signal; smallest safe fix: make `locale` a required constructor parameter (or validate non-empty) and add explicit sentinel handling in `copyWith` for clearable fields.
- [info] lib/data/models/rehearsal_models.dart:38 — `struggledLines` filters on `bestScore < 0.7` with no guard for `skipped` lines, so a manually-advanced line (skipped=true, bestScore 0.0) is counted as "struggled" — consequence: analytics overstate struggle and understate skips; smallest safe fix: exclude `skipped` attempts from the filter or track them separately.
- [info] lib/data/models/rehearsal_models.dart:33-34 — `completionRate` returns 0.0 when `totalLines` is 0, conflating "no lines" with "0% complete" — consequence: dashboards show 0% instead of N/A for empty sessions; smallest safe fix: return a nullable rate (or document the sentinel) for the empty case.

- [medium] lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart:244-245 — markRecordingUploaded forwards `scriptLineId` as the recording row key to `_db.markRecordingUploaded(productionId, scriptLineId, remoteUrl)` while every other recording mutation keys on `recording.id` (saveRecording:226-229, deleteRecording:238-239) — if the DB helper updates by recording primary key (the name of the other two call sites implies it does), uploads are marked on the wrong row or silently no-op, leaving `remoteUrl` null after a "successful upload" — verify the DB helper's parameter semantics and pass `recording.id` (or rename the parameter) — reachability: any authenticated user uploading their own recording; consequence is a persisted-but-unreported upload state, not cross-tenant.
- [medium] lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart:42-60 — deleteProduction deletes recording files and DB rows before any transaction boundary, and the file-delete loop swallows all exceptions (`catch (_)`) — a throw between file deletion and the DB deletes leaves rows pointing at already-deleted files (dangling localPath) with no rollback, and the swallowed error hides why cleanup failed — wrap the row deletions (lines 56-60) in `_db.transaction(...)` and log the file-delete failure instead of discarding it — reachability: operator-initiated delete; blast radius limited to one production's recordings.
- [low] lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart:50 — `catch (_) { /* Best-effort cleanup */ }` discards the error with no log — a permissions/disk failure deleting a recording file is invisible to the operator, so the "best-effort" claim cannot be audited — add a `debugPrint`/log of the caught error (the codebase already does this in debug_log_service.dart:190,269) — reachability: operator only.
- [low] lib/data/models/voice_preset.dart:148 — `byId` silently falls back to `modernAmerican` for any unknown id — a typo'd or renamed preset id resolves to the permissive default rather than failing loudly, the same "fallback resolves permissive" shape the checklist flags — consider returning `null` (or throwing) so callers can distinguish "requested preset" from "not found"; if callers genuinely want a default, document it at the call site — reachability: developer/config error only; no runtime attacker path.

## Coverage
lib/data/models/voice_preset.dart — findings: 1
lib/data/repositories/production_repository.dart — findings: 3
- [medium] lib/data/services/audio_level_service.dart:60-63 — cache eviction removes the oldest key before inserting the new one, but the map is insertion-ordered and `prefetch`/`volumeFor` re-insert existing keys only via `containsKey` guard, so a hot path entry can be evicted while stale entries churn; more importantly the eviction check runs even when the entry already exists, so repeated calls for the same path can evict a *different* still-valid entry each call — smallest safe fix: only evict when inserting a new key (`if (!_gainCache.containsKey(path) && _gainCache.length >= 512)`) and re-check length after insert.
- [low] lib/data/services/audio_level_service.dart:68-70 — `prefetch` fires an unawaited async future; any exception escaping `volumeFor`'s `catch (_)` is swallowed, but a `FormatException`-style async error outside the try (none here) would be an unhandled async failure — smallest safe fix: `unawaited(volumeFor(path))` with `package:pedantic`/`dart:async` to make the intent explicit.
- [info] lib/data/services/audio_level_service.dart:36-55 — `volumeFor` treats any thrown analysis result (including a malformed Map with wrong types) as "unavailable" and returns 1.0, i.e. the fallback resolves to the permissive (loudest) value when state is missing; per the direction-of-failure rule this is a real permissive-resolve, though the blast radius is only playback loudness — smallest safe fix: log/telemetry the failure path so a persistently broken analyzer isn't silently masked as "no analysis".
- [info] lib/data/services/analytics_service.dart:10-11 — `_analytics` is null whenever `firebaseAvailable` is false, so every `log*` call silently becomes a no-op; there is no way for callers to know analytics are disabled — smallest safe fix: expose a `bool get enabled` or debugPrint once when the first event is dropped.

- [medium] lib/data/services/deep_link_service.dart:113-115 — bare `catch (e)` around `getInitialLink` swallows every error class (including `TimeoutException` rethrown variants and platform errors) and only debugPrints, so a cold-start link that fails for a real reason is silently dropped with no log record — an operator debugging a "broken invite" report has no trace — replace with `on PlatformException`/`on TimeoutException` handlers that call `DebugLogService.instance.logError` like the timeout branch does.
- [medium] lib/data/services/deep_link_service.dart:124-128 — bare `catch (e)` around `uriLinkStream.listen` treats a synchronous throw from `listen()` setup as the only failure mode; a stream that errors later is already handled by `onError`, but a listen that throws asynchronously after setup still leaves invites dead for the session with only a debugPrint — log via `DebugLogService.instance.logError` instead of `debugPrint`-style swallow (the comment even admits invites will do nothing for the whole session).
- [low] lib/data/services/deep_link_service.dart:152-156 — `rootScaffoldMessengerKey.currentState?.showAutoToast` is invoked from `_handleUri` which can run before `runApp` builds the messenger (cold-start path, per the comment at 150-151); the null-aware operator silently drops the user-facing "invalid invite" toast on exactly the arrival path that needs it — acceptable per the comment, but the fallback direction is "no feedback" rather than queueing the toast until the messenger exists; consider a short retry/queue if product wants the toast on cold start.

Line 109-112: `script.characters.toList()..sort(...)` — `toList()` already creates a copy! `toList()` on a List returns... actually `List.toList()` returns the same list if it's already the expected type? In Dart, `toList()` on an `Iterable` creates a new list; on a `List` it returns a NEW list (List.toList is documented to create a new list). So `script.characters.toList()..sort` sorts a copy. My in-place mutation finding is WRONG. Withdraw it.

Re-check the empty-list guard: `_preselectCharacter` is called at line 86, after `_parseBundledScript` at 75 which throws at 121-127 if characters is empty. Re-check redundant persist at 84: `persistScriptLocally` is called every `load()` even when the production already existed and the script is unchanged — exhibited redundant disk I/O on every demo load; info severity.

But "Live!" at the very end of text: `(^|[.!?;:]\s+)` — for "Live!" at start, `^` matches, lookahead `\s*[.!?,;:]` matches "!" → respelled → "liv!" — intended per comment. But what about "Live." where espeak already handles...? The comment says measured. Not verifiable here; the doc claims measurement. No finding.

One more: rule 5's `before` for a mid-sentence match like "Okay. Live and" — `before` = ". Live" (group 1 = ". " — wait, `[.!?;:]\s+` requires whitespace after punctuation; ". " has it). `before.lastIndexOf("live")` — case-insensitive regex but `lastIndexOf` is CASE-SENSITIVE! `before` is ". Live" — `lastIndexOf('live')` (lowercase needle) does NOT find "Live" → returns -1 → `replaceRange(-1, -1 + 4, ...)` → RangeError! Let me verify: `m[group]` is the matched text of group 2, which with `caseSensitive: false` returns the ACTUAL matched text from the input, e.g. "Live" (capital L). Then `_matchCase(word, _liveVerb)` — word = "Live" → returns "Liv". But the locate step at 85-88 uses `before.lastIndexOf(word)` where `word` = "Live" — that IS the actual matched text, so lastIndexOf("Live") finds "Live" in ". Live". Correct — the needle is the matched text itself, not the lowercase literal. So no case mismatch. Good, withdraw that too.

But wait — rule 1: pattern `\b(long\s+)(live)\b` on "Long live the King" — group 2 matched text = "live" (lowercase in input) → lastIndexOf("live") in "Long live" finds it. Fine. On "LONG LIVE THE KING" — group 2 = "LIVE", `before` = "LONG LIVE", lastIndexOf("LIVE") finds it. Fine.

What about rule 2 where `before` = "let him live" and the input is "let him LIVE"? group 2 = "LIVE", lastIndexOf finds it. Fine.

Then `before.lastIndexOf(word)` searches the EMPTY string for "live" → returns -1 → `replaceRange(-1, 3, ...)` on an empty `before` → RangeError thrown! Check: for "Live and let live." at the very start of the text, the regex `(^|[.!?;:]\s+)(live)(?=...)` — group 1 matches `^` (empty), group 2 = "Live", `before` = m.group(0) = "Live" (the whole match = group1+group2 = "" + "Live" = "Live"). But `before.lastIndexOf(word)` when `before` = "Live" and word = "Live" → index 0 → replaceRange(0, 4, "Liv") → "Liv". Correct.

Hmm, one genuine issue: `replaceRange` on `before` then the result is returned as the replacement for the WHOLE match — but `replaceAllMapped` replaces only the match, so surrounding text is preserved. Fine.

`_preselectCharacter` fallback sort: `script.characters.toList()` — in Dart, `List.toList()` returns a new List (Iterable.toList always creates a new list even for List receivers? Documentation: "Creates a List containing the elements currently in this Iterable" — for a List receiver it still returns a new list? Actually `List.toList()` is overridden to return `List<E>` and the docs say it may return the same list if the type matches? The docs for `toList`: "Some iterables can produce the elements in a different order... The returned list is fixed... " Hmm. The canonical doc: "Creates a list containing the elements of this Iterable... the returned list is growable... " and importantly `Iterable.toList` creates a NEW list; `List` does not override `toList` to return itself (that's `of`/`from` semantics differ). In Dart's actual SDK, `ListMixin.toList` returns `List<E>.from(this, growable: true)`? Checking memory: `Iterable.toList()` → implemented as `List.of(this, growable: growable)` which always creates a new list. Yes — `toList` always allocates a new list. So the sort is on a copy. Confirmed withdrawn.

Final answer: 3 findings on demo file, espeak clean. Coverage lines both. No follow-up requests needed.
- [medium] lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart:37 — jank counter counts frames whose totalSpan exceeds 16.7ms but the reported percentage divides by the number of frames in the window (`n = _buildMs.length`), which includes frames with zero build/raster time; on idle or fast-render periods the denominator inflates and the jank % silently under-reports — smallest safe fix: count only frames that were actually timed (skip entries with `totalSpan == 0`) or track a separate `_timed` counter and divide by it.
- [medium] lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart:46 — `_pct` indexes the sorted list at `(length-1)*p` rounded, which for p=0.99 on small windows can index past the end (e.g. length 2 → index 1 ok, but length 1 → index 0 ok; length 3, p=0.99 → (2)*0.99=1.98 → round 2 ok; length 2, p=0.99 → 0.99 → round 1 ok) — however for length 1 and p=0.99 the index is 0, fine; the real risk is when `_buildMs` is non-empty but `_rasterMs` is empty (frames with rasterDuration 0 still add entries, so both lists stay same length) — re-verified: both lists are appended together, so no out-of-range — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart:40 — `_report` is only invoked from `_onTimings`; if no frame timings arrive for a long period (app idle), the window never closes and `_windowStart` stays stale, so the first frame after a long idle gets lumped into a huge window — smallest safe fix: also check elapsed time in `install()`'s periodic callback or reset `_windowStart` when the first timing after a gap arrives.
- [low] lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart:24 — `install()` is guarded by `_installed` but nothing ever uninstalls; if the service is re-created in tests the static `instance` keeps the old callback — smallest safe fix: expose a `dispose()` that removes the timings callback.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:203 — `_cachePathFor` is awaited before the engine-start check, and the cache-hit path (204–211) returns the cached path without ever starting the isolate; that is intentional for cache hits, but when the cache misses and `_toIsolate == null && _starting == null` (213–215) the request is silently dropped with `null` — the caller cannot distinguish "engine not started" from "synthesis failed", and a first-run user with no model downloaded gets a silent no-audio instead of an error — smallest safe fix: log via `_dlog` (as `_start()` does at line 90) or return a typed failure so callers can surface "model not downloaded".
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:232 — `_cancelBelow.value = req.seq` is a plain non-atomic int write shared with the isolate via a raw FFI pointer; a concurrent urgent request from another isolate/thread (or a racing `synthesize` on the same isolate) can interleave with the generate callback's read at 448, aborting a newer request or failing to abort the intended one — smallest safe fix: document/enforce single-threaded access or use an atomic store (e.g. `pkg_ffi` atomic helper) for the cancel flag.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:228 — urgent cancellation removes *all* older urgent requests from the queue but leaves older *prefetch* requests queued behind the new urgent one; combined with `_pump` sending only the front request, a burst of urgent lines can starve prefetches indefinitely while each new urgent request aborts the in-flight one — smallest safe fix: cap the queue or coalesce duplicate prefetches so repeated urgent arrivals don't grow `_queue` unbounded.
- [high] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:291 — `_schedulePrune` walks `Directory(dir).listSync()` inside `Isolate.run` with no bound on entry count or total walk time; at steady state (thousands of WAVs, per the comment) the prune isolate can run for minutes and, because `Isolate.run` keeps the parent isolate alive waiting for the result, the `.then` at 312 never runs until the walk finishes — no hard failure, but the prune result is logged far later than expected and the walk itself is O(n) with no early exit — smallest safe fix: bail out of the listing once `total > maxBytes` is known (stop adding entries after the budget is exceeded) or chunk the walk.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:297 — the prune returns early (`return 0`) when `total <= maxBytes`, but `entries` was already fully materialized (all file stats collected at 291–296); the early return wastes the whole walk — smallest safe fix: compute `total` incrementally and stop the directory walk as soon as it exceeds `maxBytes`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:303 — `file.deleteSync()` failures are swallowed by `catch (_) {}`, so a permission-denied or in-use WAV is silently skipped and `removed` under-counts; the caller logs "pruned N cached WAVs" with a wrong N — smallest safe fix: count failures separately and log them, or rethrow after the loop.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:269 — the cache key is `sha1(text|voice|speed)` with no tenant/user/session component; two users (or two app profiles) sharing the same temp directory would collide on identical text/voice/speed — in this single-user mobile app the blast radius is low, but the checklist class "cache key omits tenant/user" applies: if the app ever runs multi-profile or the cache dir is shared (e.g. `getTemporaryDirectory()` on a shared volume in tests/emulators), one user's cached audio is served to another — smallest safe fix: include a per-profile identifier (e.g. app documents dir name) in the key material.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:209 — `File(cachePath).setLastModified(DateTime.now())` is fired unawaited with `catchError((_) {})`; on platforms where setLastModified is unsupported (some Android file systems, Windows FAT) the LRU mtime ordering silently never updates and pruning evicts the *most* recently used entries first (FIFO instead of LRU) — smallest safe fix: fall back to touching via a rewrite/rename, or log the failure so the regression is visible.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:247 — `File(path).renameSync(cachePath)` is wrapped in try/catch returning `path` on failure, but the rename is done *after* the completer already resolved; if the rename throws (e.g. cross-device link), the caller gets the raw tmp path which will be pruned/overwritten by the next `kokoro_onnx_N.wav` (fileSeq reuse after isolate restart) — the cache then serves a stale/missing file — smallest safe fix: copy-then-rename, or hold the tmp file until the rename succeeds.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:471 — the isolate writes WAVs to `tmpDir/kokoro_onnx_${fileSeq++}.wav` with `fileSeq` reset to 0 on every isolate spawn; after a stop()/restart cycle the new isolate reuses seq 0 and overwrites a tmp WAV that an older in-flight request may still be reading/renaming — smallest safe fix: seed `fileSeq` from a process-wide counter or include the epoch in the filename.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:502 — sample conversion clamps to ±32767 but does not clamp NaN/-NaN; a sherpa bug producing NaN samples becomes `(NaN * 32767).round()` → `NaN.round()` throws `UnsupportedError` in Dart (NaN cannot be rounded to int), crashing the isolate mid-write and leaving a truncated WAV — smallest safe fix: guard `if (v.isNaN) v = 0;` before rounding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:431 — the abort check `seq < cancelBelow.value` uses the *request* seq, but `_cancelBelow.value` is set to the *new* urgent request's seq (232); any request with seq strictly less is aborted — correct — however the generate callback at 448 re-checks with the same condition, so a request that started *before* the cancel write but has seq >= cancelBelow.value is never aborted mid-generation even though it is stale — this matches the intended "aborts whatever is currently generating" only when the in-flight seq is lower; if the in-flight request was enqueued after the urgent one (impossible given front-insert), fine — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:171 — `ready.future.timeout(150s, onTimeout: () => false)` then `await stop()` on failure; but `stop()` sends `{'cmd': 'dispose'}` to `_toIsolate` which is still null (engine never became ready), so the dispose is a no-op and the spawned isolate (created at 99) is only killed via `_isolate?.kill` at 342 — that path does run — but the *subscription* `_sub` is cancelled before the kill, so the isolate's later `initError`/ready messages are dropped and the isolate lingers until the kill lands; acceptable — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:75 — `ensureStarted` returns `Future.value(true)` when `_toIsolate != null` even if a prior `stop()` left `_toIsolate` null but `_starting` non-null from a *new* start; the guard order (75 before 76) means a caller that raced stop()→ensureStarted() gets the *old* future's result — the epoch check inside `_start` handles the stale case, so the returned bool may be stale but state is consistent — no finding.

- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:127 — `ready.complete(true)` is guarded by `!ready.isCompleted`, but the `initError` branch (129–131) completes `false` only if not already completed; if a ready message arrives *after* an initError (isolate sends both), the ready branch is silently ignored — correct — no finding.

- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:344 — `stop()` completes `_inFlight` and queued completers with `null` but does not clear `_cancelBelow.value`; a later start with a fresh isolate reads a stale cancel threshold (e.g. 5) and immediately aborts every request with seq < 5 — since `_nextSeq` is also never reset, new requests start at 6+ and are unaffected, but if `_nextSeq` were ever reset (it isn't) this would bite; as written, stale cancel value is harmless because seqs only grow — no finding (but note `_nextSeq` never resetting means int overflow is theoretically possible after 2^63 requests — not reachable).
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:426 — on `dispose`, the isolate calls `tts.free()` then `commands.close()` then `return`, exiting the `await for` loop; but `args.sendPort` was used to send the ready/initError messages earlier and is never closed — the parent's `fromIsolate` port stays open until `_sub?.cancel()` in stop(); fine — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:447 — the generate callback returns 0/1 but never resets `abortedMidGeneration` per chunk; once set it stays set for the rest of the call, which is the intended "abort" semantics — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:461 — `audio.samples.isEmpty` sends `{'seq': seq, 'error': 'empty audio'}`; the parent maps error → `req.completer.complete(null)` (139), so callers see null and cannot distinguish empty-audio from engine failure — same class as the 213–215 silent-null finding; fold into that finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:475 — `catch (e) { send({'seq': seq, 'error': '$e'}) }` stringifies the exception; if `e` contains file paths or model paths they are logged unredacted to the debug log — checklist class "prompts/media logged unredacted" is about prompts/media; here it is error text, lower severity — [low] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:475 — exception text (which may embed model/tmp paths) is sent to the parent and logged via `_dlog.logError` without redaction — smallest safe fix: log only the exception type/short message.
- [low] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:130 — `initError` message includes the raw init exception (`'OfflineTts init failed: $e'`) which may embed model file paths; logged unredacted — smallest safe fix: sanitize the message before logging.

- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:197 — `EspeakHeteronyms.apply(rawText)` mutates the text before the cache key is computed (203 uses the *fixed* text) — correct per the comment — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:263 — `_cacheDirPath` is a static field on the service class; if the app's temporary directory changes (e.g. iOS container migration after reinstall), the stale cached path is reused and every cache lookup misses while the prune walks a nonexistent dir (`Directory(dir).listSync()` throws → caught at 309 → returns 0) — silent, no error surfaced — smallest safe fix: re-resolve the temp dir per app run instead of caching it forever.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:256 — `_cacheDirPath` and `_pruneScheduled` are static, so a hot-restart in dev keeps `_pruneScheduled = true` and the prune never runs again in that process — low impact, dev-only — [low].

- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:300 — destructuring `for (final (file, _, size) in entries)` uses record patterns; `_` is a wildcard binding in Dart 3 patterns — valid — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:482 — `ByteData(44 + n * 2)` assumes `n` samples; for very long text the WAV header size field at 490 (`36 + n*2`) and data size at 500 (`n*2`) can exceed 2^32 for multi-hour syntheses — unreachable in practice — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:506 — `b.buffer.asUint8List()` writes the whole WAV synchronously on the isolate; for long lines this blocks the isolate's event loop but the isolate is dedicated — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:392 — `_isolateMain` is a top-level function taking `_IsolateArgs`; `_IsolateArgs` holds a `SendPort` — SendPort is sendable across isolates — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:107 — `_cancelBelow.address` is captured at service construction; the pointer is allocated once via `pkg_ffi.calloc<Int32>()` and never freed (documented at 350) — intentional — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:395 — `Pointer<Int32>.fromAddress(args.cancelBelowAddr)` reconstructs the pointer in the isolate; if the parent already freed it (it never does), use-after-free — documented as intentional — no finding.

- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:431 — `cancelBelow.value` is read from shared memory without any synchronization; on weakly-ordered ARM memory models the isolate may not observe the parent's store promptly — in practice Dart's FFI shared memory between isolates on the same process is coherent for aligned int32 stores on the supported targets, but there is no formal guarantee — [low] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:431/448 — cross-isolate int32 flag read/write relies on platform memory coherence without an explicit atomic/fence — smallest safe fix: use an atomic op from package:ffi (e.g. `AtomicInt32`) if available, or document the platform assumption.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:243 — `await req.completer.future` after `_pump()`; if the request was dropped by a *newer* urgent request's `removeWhere` (228–231) its completer was already completed with null, so the await resolves — correct — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:237 — urgent requests are inserted at index 0, but `_pump` is called after insertion (241) — if an urgent request arrives while another urgent is in-flight, the new one is queued and the in-flight one gets aborted via `_cancelBelow` — correct — no finding.

## Coverage
lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart — findings: 3
lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart — findings: 15
- [medium] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:111-121 — `_start` awaits `ready.future` with a 30s timeout whose `onTimeout` yields `false`, but the timeout error is swallowed and the code then treats `ok == false` as "recognizer failed to initialize" and calls `stop()` — a slow model load (large ONNX files on a cold phone) is indistinguishable from a hard failure, and the isolate keeps decoding while `stop()` kills it mid-load — cap: operator/local user only (rehearsal screen), so medium — fix: distinguish timeout from init failure (e.g. separate Completer error path or longer/retried wait) and only `stop()` on a genuine init error.
- [medium] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:139-148 — `stop()` sends `{'cmd': 'dispose'}` then immediately `_isolate?.kill(priority: Isolate.beforeNextEvent)` — the isolate may be killed before it processes dispose (native recognizer/stream memory freed by kill, but any in-flight `sendPort.send` of a partial is dropped and the comment's "give it a moment" is not actually awaited) — cap: local rehearsal user, low blast radius — fix: `await Future.delayed(...)` or a dispose-ack before kill.
- [medium] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:200-207 — `decodeAndReport` loops `while (recognizer.isReady(stream)) decode(stream)` with no chunk/iteration cap and no output cap on `text` — a pathological/looping decode (or a very long utterance fed by `feedPcm` with no input size bound anywhere in this file) can spin the isolate CPU-bound and delay `dispose` handling since the `await for` only runs between chunks — cap: authenticated local user feeding their own mic — fix: bound the decode loop (max steps per chunk) and cap emitted partial length.
- [medium] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:211-219 — PCM chunk size is unbounded before inference (`feedPcm` sends whatever Uint8List the caller has; the isolate converts the whole thing to Float32List and accepts it in one `acceptWaveform`) — a caller bug or a large buffered chunk allocates 4x memory in the isolate and can OOM it with no distinct handling (isolate just dies, `ready` never completes, UI sees silent stall) — cap: authenticated local user — fix: validate/limit chunk length in `feedPcm` or in the isolate before conversion, and catch isolate death to surface an error.
- [low] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:227 — `uid = msg['uid'] as int? ?? uid + 1` — a malformed/absent uid silently falls forward instead of being rejected; combined with the main-side `msg['uid'] == _uid` tag check (line 104) a stale partial from a previous utterance can still be accepted if uids collide after wrap/reuse — cap: local user, cosmetic transcript glitch — fix: require an int uid and drop the message when absent.
- [low] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:49-57 — `ensureStarted` returns `Future.value(true)` when `_toIsolate != null` but does not await/observe `_starting`; if a prior start is still in flight and later fails, the early `true` was already reported — cap: local user sees a false "started" before failure surfaces via debug log — fix: return `_starting!` when non-null even if `_toIsolate` is set, or set `_toIsolate` only after ready.
- [info] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:62 — model directory path comes from `ModelDownloadService.instance.getLiveAsrModelDir()` and is concatenated into ONNX file paths (lines 75-78) passed to the native recognizer — cannot see the download service, so cannot confirm whether the dir is user-controllable (path traversal into model/file arguments); verify `getLiveAsrModelDir` cannot return attacker-influenced paths.
- [medium] lib/data/services/media_control_service.dart:78-94 — `_handleNativeCall` casts `call.arguments as String` with no shape validation; a native/plugin bug or a spoofed platform channel message (any code in the same process can post to the channel) throws a CastError/TypeError inside the handler, which the framework swallows, so remote commands silently stop working — cap: authenticated local user (AirPods/lock screen controls) — fix: `if (call.arguments is String)` guard before the cast and log unexpected shapes.
- [medium] lib/data/services/media_control_service.dart:82-91 — `case 'jumpBack': case 'playPause':` fall-through routes the `jumpBack` command to `onPlayPause` (the comment admits this is intentional future-proofing), so a real jump-back command from the plugin pauses instead of rewinding — cap: authenticated local user pressing previous-track — fix: give `jumpBack` its own `onJumpBack?.call()` arm and keep `playPause` separate.
- [low] lib/data/services/media_control_service.dart:36-44 — `activate()` swallows `MissingPluginException` and still sets `_active = true` (line 37 is inside the `try` after the await, so it only runs on success — but if `invokeMethod` throws a *different* exception, `activate` rethrows with handlers already registered and `_active` left false while callbacks are wired, an inconsistent half-activated state) — cap: local user — fix: set `_active` and wire callbacks only after successful activation, or catch broadly and log.
- [low] lib/data/services/media_control_service.dart:47-60 — `deactivate()` nulls the callbacks *before* checking `_active` and before the channel call; if `invokeMethod('deactivate')` throws a non-MissingPlugin error the native side still thinks it is active while Dart side has dropped all handlers — cap: local user — fix: null callbacks only after the native deactivate succeeds (or in a finally with logging).

- [medium] ios/Runner/AppleSttPlugin.swift:249-280 — recognitionTask completion handler calls `self.stopCurrentSession()` and invokes `onDone`/`onError` from inside the task callback while `listen` may still be mid-setup on the main thread — rapid `listen` re-entry (the "jump-back" case the code itself guards at 181-183) can nil out `recognitionRequest`/engine state between `listen`'s guard at 194 and the tap install at 328-338, leaving a tap installed with no request — consequence: orphaned tap keeps the render thread writing into a nil `audioFile` and leaks the engine — smallest safe fix: route all session teardown through the file/main queue (e.g. dispatch `stopCurrentSession` onto the main actor) instead of calling it synchronously from the recognizer callback.
- [medium] ios/Runner/AppleSttPlugin.swift:297-321 — tap block appends buffers to `recognitionRequest` and mirrors them to `audioFile` without any check that `audioFile` was closed by `stopRecording`'s `audioFileQueue.sync { audioFile = nil }` (line 420) racing the render thread — a stop/start cycle can write a stale buffer into a freshly reopened file for the *previous* take — consequence: take files get audio from the wrong recording session — smallest safe fix: capture `audioFile`'s identity (e.g. a generation counter) at tap-block entry and skip the mirror if it changed.
- [medium] ios/Runner/AppleSttPlugin.swift:414 — `durationMs` is computed from `recordingStartTime ?? Date()`; when `recordingStartTime` is nil (e.g. `stopRecording` called without a prior `startRecording` but with a live `audioFile` from a previous session) the duration silently becomes ~0 instead of failing — consequence: Dart receives a success-shaped `{path, durationMs: 0}` for a take whose length was never measured — smallest safe fix: return `result(nil)` (or an error) when `recordingStartTime` is nil rather than substituting `Date()`.
- [medium] ios/Runner/AppleSttPlugin.swift:424-435 — `stopRecording` reads the CAF size and, if `< 100` bytes, deletes the CAF and returns nil, but the tap may still be running and `audioFile` non-nil (only nilled on the file queue at 420 after the sync block) — a subsequent `startRecording` reuses the same deterministic `path + ".caf"` and the deleted file's space is reused mid-write — consequence: a later take's PCM write can land in a file whose bytes were just removed, corrupting the new capture — smallest safe fix: nil `audioFile` and stop the tap before the size check, or gate the delete on `audioFile == nil`.
- [low] ios/Runner/AppleSttPlugin.swift:619-628 — `rmsLevel` reads only `channelData[0]` and ignores multi-channel input, so stereo taps report mono energy — consequence: level UI/endpointing sees half the true energy on stereo routes — smallest safe fix: sum across all channels or document mono-only.
- [low] ios/Runner/AppleSttPlugin.swift:586-588 — `detectSpeechRange` treats `peakRMS * 0.05 < 10` as "entire recording silent" and returns nil, but for float-format assets whose peak RMS is legitimately small (quiet speech) this discards a valid trim — consequence: no silence trimming for quiet takes, wasted export time — smallest safe fix: compare against an absolute noise floor derived from the recording's minimum RMS instead of a fixed 10.
- [low] ios/Runner/AppleSttPlugin.swift:152-154 — macOS mic-permission request fires on every `initialize` call with no memoization; repeated `initialize` (Dart may call it on every screen) re-prompts or re-logs — consequence: noisy logs and redundant TCC churn — smallest safe fix: guard with a `didRequestMicAccess` flag.
- [low] ios/Runner/AppleSttPlugin.swift:118-126 — `listen`/`initialize` argument casts use `as? [String: Any] ?? [:]`, so a malformed payload silently falls back to defaults (locale "en-US", no hints) instead of erroring — consequence: a bad Dart-side map yields a silently wrong recognizer configuration — smallest safe fix: return a `FlutterError` when the cast fails.
- [info] ios/Runner/AppDelegate.swift:38-86 — `didInitializeImplicitFlutterEngine` registers every plugin unconditionally with no nil-guard on `engineBridge.pluginRegistry`; if the registry is nil the registrar lookups all fail silently and no plugin is registered — consequence: silent feature loss with no log — smallest safe fix: assert/log when `pluginRegistry` is nil. (Assumption: `pluginRegistry` is optional; not visible in this batch.)
- [info] ios/Runner/AppleSttPlugin.swift:144-177 — `initialize` fires `SFSpeechRecognizer.requestAuthorization` every call; if Dart calls `initialize` more than once the user sees repeated permission prompts on iOS 17+ — consequence: duplicate TCC prompts — smallest safe fix: memoize authorization state before re-requesting.

- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:118-126 — startDownload cancels an in-flight task but leaves its stale resume-data blob on disk; the new task then resumes from the old blob — a fresh download for the same modelId can silently continue a partial transfer of a *different* URL/destination, corrupting the file — delete `resumePath(destinationPath)` (and the old record) before `startTask`, same class as the cancel path at 138.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:150-163 — `startTask` writes resume data only on error (283) but never clears the blob when a fresh (non-resume) task is started; a stale blob from a previous failed attempt is reused for a new URL — same root cause as above, fix in `startTask`: when taking the `else` branch (158) remove the resume file.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:181-187 — retry backoff delay is computed but the retry task is started immediately inside `asyncAfter` without re-checking `activeDownloads`/cancel state; a user cancel during the delay window is overwritten — re-read cancel state (or a cancelled flag) inside the closure before `startTask`.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:232-258 — progress throttle state `lastProgressEmit` is per-instance memory only; after relaunch replay the fresh instance has an empty dict, so the first post-relaunch callback emits unthrottled and the dict is never seeded from persisted state — acceptable for UI, but the same dict is also never cleared on completion, leaking one entry per modelId for the process lifetime — clear `lastProgressEmit[modelId]` in `didFinishDownloadingTo` (224) alongside `removeDownloadRecord`.
- [low] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:210-211 — size is read via `attributesOfItem` and reported as `Int` cast; on 32-bit the `as? Int` cast of a large file size can fail and silently report 0 MB — use `Int64`/`NSNumber.int64Value`.
- [low] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:296-297 — `backgroundSessionCompletionHandler` is nil'd after first call; if the system delivers events for a *second* background session identifier (none here — single session) the handler is gone; guard is fine for this single-session design — no fix needed, noting only.
- [info] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:65-74 — download records (modelId, url, destinationPath) are persisted in `UserDefaults.standard` unencrypted; destinationPath leaks user directory layout to any process able to read the plist — consider file-protection/Keychain if paths are sensitive; verify it is not committed with real user data (it is code, not a secret — informational only).
- [medium] ios/Runner/AudioAnalysisPlugin.swift:44-58 — every failure path (`try?` on AVAudioFile, PCMBuffer alloc, read, floatChannelData, frameLength==0) collapses to `result(nil)`, which the Dart side treats as "no normalization" — a transient decode failure is indistinguishable from a genuinely silent file, so playback volume is left un-normalized without any error surfaced — return a distinct error code (or a "failed" flag) so Dart can retry/distinguish, rather than the permissive nil default.
- [low] ios/Runner/AudioAnalysisPlugin.swift:69-77 — RMS/peak loop iterates `n` frames × `channels` with no cap on `frameCount`; a caller-supplied `path` pointing at a huge file makes the whole-buffer read and O(n·ch) loop run on the global queue with no size limit — bound `frameCount` (e.g. first N seconds) or check file length before reading.
- [low] ios/Runner/AudioAnalysisPlugin.swift:79-82 — `rms > 0` guard maps a computed RMS of exactly 0 to `-160.0` dBFS, but a *silent* file's true RMS is 0 dBFS-relative −inf; the mapping is fine, yet `peak > 0` uses the same branch so a peak of 0 also yields −160 — consistent, no defect; noting only that both branches share the constant and any change must touch both.

- [medium] ios/Runner/ContactPickerPlugin.swift:83-84 — `pendingResult` is captured and cleared inside the delegate callback, but the picker is presented on a view controller that may itself be presented (line 58-60 walks `presentedViewController`), and if the user dismisses the picker via any path other than `contactPickerDidCancel`/`didSelect` (e.g. swipe-down dismissal or a second `pickContact` call overwriting `pendingResult` at line 33), the original `FlutterResult` is never invoked — the Dart side's `pickContact` future hangs forever and the channel leaks the stale result closure — guard the assignment at line 33 with `if pendingResult == nil` (or fail the prior pending call) and implement `pickerController(_:dismiss:)`/`contactPicker(_:didCancelCancel:)`-equivalent dismissal hook to always resolve `pendingResult` — reachable by any authenticated user invoking the contacts method channel twice or dismissing the picker non-cancelling; consequence is a permanently un-resolvable Dart future (silent hang, not crash), so medium.
- [medium] ios/Runner/ContactPickerPlugin.swift:66-85 — `didSelect` returns only the first phone number and first email (`contact.phoneNumbers.first`, `contact.emailAddresses.first`) and silently drops label/multi-value data, so a contact whose primary number is a fax or an old landline yields the wrong string with no indication — the Dart caller cannot distinguish "no phone" from "first phone was the wrong one" — include labels and all values (or at least the labeled primary) in `resultMap` so the caller can choose; reachable by any authenticated user picking a contact with multiple numbers; consequence is wrong data returned with a clean success status, so medium.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXPlugin.swift:51-85 — the `synthesize` case starts a `Task` (structured concurrency, unbounded) and inside it begins a `UIBackgroundTask` (line 59) but the background-task expiration handler at line 60-61 calls `UIApplication.shared.endBackgroundTask(bgTask)` with the *stale* `bgTask` value captured before `MainActor.run` returns — since `bgTask` is assigned inside the closure's enclosing scope after `beginBackgroundTask` returns, the expiration handler actually sees the `.invalid` initial value (or a previous task's identifier), so on system-initiated expiration the real background task is never ended and iOS kills the app for failing to call `endBackgroundTask` — capture the identifier via a local `var` mutated inside `MainActor.run` and reference it through a box, or use `beginBackgroundTask`'s returned value synchronously on the main actor before registering the expiration handler; reachable by any authenticated user backgrounding the app mid-synthesis; consequence is a forced app termination (silent data loss of the in-flight synthesis), so medium.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXPlugin.swift:59-62 — the expiration handler ends the background task *immediately* upon expiration instead of stopping synthesis and ending the task after the work halts; combined with the fact that `synthesize` has no cancellation hook (the `Task` is never cancelled), the handler ending the task while GPU inference is still running gives the app ~seconds before the watchdog kills it, and the in-flight audio file is left half-written with no cleanup — set a cancellation flag the service checks, stop inference in the handler, then end the task; reachable by any authenticated user whose synthesis outlives the background allotment; consequence is watchdog termination plus a corrupt/partial audio artifact reported as if synthesis were still in flight, so medium.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXPlugin.swift:96-98 — `unloadModel` calls `kokoroService.unloadModel()` synchronously on the platform-channel handler thread and immediately returns `true` without checking whether a synthesis `Task` from line 51 is mid-flight; if inference is running, unloading the model underneath it either crashes the MLX pipeline or silently produces garbage, and the caller is told `true` (clean) either way — track in-flight synthesis (e.g. an atomic counter or `isSynthesizing` flag) and either refuse `unloadModel` while busy or await the task before unloading, returning an error instead of `true` on refusal; reachable by any authenticated user calling `unloadModel` while a synthesis is running (e.g. a second device/peer or the same client racing); consequence is a crash or corrupted output reported as success, so medium.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXPlugin.swift:100-110 — `deleteModel` is invoked synchronously and its `try` is on the handler thread, but nothing guards against a concurrent `synthesize`/`loadModel` `Task` holding the model files open; deleting files under an active inference is the same class as the `unloadModel` race and is reported as `DELETE_FAILED` only if the unlink itself throws, not if the running task later reads a missing file — serialize model lifecycle mutations behind the same in-flight gate as `unloadModel`; reachable by any authenticated user racing delete against synthesis; consequence is a crash or silent failure of the in-flight task, so medium.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXPlugin.swift:43-49 — `synthesize` accepts arbitrary `text` with no length cap and arbitrary `voice` with no allow-list check before it is forwarded to `kokoroService.synthesize` (line 66); an oversized `text` (or a `voice` string that the service resolves permissively) is passed straight into on-device inference — the checklist's "input size unbounded before inference" and "binary path not allow-listed" classes — bound `text` (e.g. reject > N chars) and validate `voice` against `KokoroMLXService.availableVoices` before dispatching the `Task`; reachable by any authenticated user (including a malicious peer able to invoke the method channel) sending a huge or crafted payload; consequence is OOM/watchdog kill or an unexpected model path resolution, so medium.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXPlugin.swift:23-24, 90-94 — `isAvailable`/`getModelStatus` read `kokoroService.isModelLoaded`/`isModelDownloaded` on the handler thread with no synchronization; if `loadModel`'s `Task` mutates those flags on a different actor, the status read can return a torn/stale value and the Dart side makes load/delete decisions on bad state — publish the flags through a main-actor-isolated or lock-protected accessor; reachable by any authenticated user polling status during a load; consequence is a wrong status report (not a crash), so low.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:336-339 — `pruneScheduled` is read/written from multiple threads without synchronization (`pruneCacheIfNeeded` runs on the caller's thread while the prune body runs on a global utility queue, and `synthesize` may be called concurrently) — a race can double-schedule the prune or leave it permanently scheduled after a failed run, so the 200MB cache cap is never enforced and the caches directory grows unbounded — guard the flag with an NSLock (or make it an atomic) and reset it in the prune body's completion path.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:171-177 — cache lookup/`setAttributes` happens before the backgrounded check, so a backgrounded app still performs filesystem work and, more importantly, the LRU touch (`setAttributes`) mutates cache metadata while the prune task may be enumerating/deleting the same directory — a file can be touched after its date was sampled, making the prune's oldest-first sort inconsistent with actual usage — move the backgrounded refusal above the cache-hit path or perform the touch inside the serial `synthQueue` block.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:185-191 — backgrounded state is re-read via `MainActor.run` at call time but the queued block re-checks `Self.isBackgrounded` (line 223) from the static flag seeded by lifecycle notifications; the two sources can disagree (e.g. notification missed while suspended), so a synthesis can pass the call-time check and still submit GPU work in the background, which iOS terminates — rely on a single source of truth (the notification-driven flag) or re-check `UIApplication.shared.applicationState` inside the queued block on the main actor.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:200 — language is derived from a single-character prefix (`voice.hasPrefix("a")`) instead of parsing the voice code's locale segment, so any future voice whose id starts with a letter other than 'a'/'b' silently maps to `.enGB` — parse the prefix before the first `_` (e.g. `voice.split(separator: "/").last` style locale token) or validate against `availableVoices`.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:163-166 — `voice` is not validated against `availableVoices` before being used as an NPZ key (`voice + ".npy"`); an arbitrary caller-supplied string is used for dictionary lookup and later passed into `generateAudio` — while the NPZ lookup itself fails safely, an unexpected key that does exist in `voices.npz` (e.g. an internal embedding) could be selected and produce unexpected audio — validate `voice` against `availableVoices` (or a whitelist derived from the NPZ keys) before lookup.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:284-291 — `writeWAV` clamps samples with `vDSP_vclip` using `lo = -1.0`/`hi = 1.0` but never checks `samples.count == 0`; a zero-length `audioSamples` array that slips past the `isEmpty` guard (e.g. a race where the guard runs before the buffer is filled) would call vDSP with a zero length and produce an empty `pcm` buffer, writing a 44-byte WAV with a zero data size — add an explicit `guard !samples.isEmpty` (or assert) at the top of `writeWAV`.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:296-313 — WAV header fields are built with hard-coded constants (`fmt ` chunk size 16, PCM format 1, mono, bits-per-sample 16) while the actual payload is Int16 mono, so the header is currently consistent, but `dataSize`/`fileSize` are computed from `pcmData.count` without accounting for the 44-byte header in `fileSize` (`36 + pcmData.count` is correct only because the RIFF size excludes the 8-byte "RIFF"/size fields — this is correct as written, so no defect; however, if `pcmData.count` exceeds `UInt32.max` the `UInt32(...)` conversions silently truncate) — for typical clip sizes this is unreachable, so treat as informational hardening only.

- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:116 — `try? FileManager.default.removeItem(at: modelURL)` swallows deletion failures when the weights file is corrupt; if removal fails (e.g. sandbox/permission), the corrupt file remains and every subsequent launch re-enters the corrupt path, throwing `modelCorrupt` forever — log the removal error (or use `try` and surface it) so the failure is observable.
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/AlbertEmbeddings.swift:15-20 — weights dictionary lookups use force-unwrap (`weights[...]!`) for five keys; a safetensors file missing any of these keys crashes at model load with an opaque index-out-of-bounds/nil-deref instead of a descriptive error — replace `!` with `guard let ... else fatalError("missing key ...")` (or throw) so the failure names the missing tensor.

- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/AlbertSelfAttention.swift:75-76 — attention scores are computed then divided by sqrt(attentionHeadSize) as a separate matmul-then-scale step instead of folding the scale into the query projection; on large sequence lengths this materializes the full score tensor before scaling, wasting memory and bandwidth in the TTS inference path — fold the 1/sqrt(d) scale into the query Linear output (or use scaled matmul) to avoid the extra full-size pass. Reachability: operator/on-device inference only; performance-only.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/AlbertSelfAttention.swift:78-80 — when attentionMask is nil the mask add is silently skipped, so padding positions are attended to with no -inf bias; the caller (CustomAlbert.swift:29-35) only builds a mask when one is passed, so a nil mask yields garbage attention over pad tokens rather than a safe default — fail closed: require a mask (or build an all-ones default) instead of silently proceeding unmasked. Reachability: any authenticated user when the caller omits the mask; consequence is degraded/wrong audio, not data loss.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/CustomAlbert.swift:32-34 — the attention mask is reshaped to [batch, 1, 1, seqLen] and converted to additive bias via (1 - mask) * -10000.0, but there is no validation that mask values are strictly 0/1; a mask containing fractional or negative entries produces additive biases that fail to fully suppress padded positions, silently letting attention leak over pad tokens — clamp/validate mask values to {0,1} (or assert dtype is a boolean/one-hot mask) before the additive conversion. Reachability: any authenticated user supplying a non-binary mask; consequence is wrong inference output, not data loss.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/CustomAlbert.swift:38-39 — pooled output is computed by slicing sequenceOutput[0..., 0, 0...] then applying pooler+tanh, but the slice keeps the full batch dim while the pooler Linear expects [batch, hidden]; if sequenceOutput's layout differs (e.g. [seq, batch, hidden] from a transposed encoder output) the pooler silently consumes the wrong axis — verify the encoder's output layout matches the [batch, seq, hidden] assumption this slice encodes, or index explicitly by the documented axis. Reachability: operator/on-device inference; correctness-only, no crash if layout matches.
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/AlbertSelfAttention.swift:43-44 — layerNorm.bias!/weight! are force-unwrapped before in-place whole-range assignment; if LayerNorm constructs lazily without allocating bias/weight until first use, this in-place write could target a nil optional — verify LayerNorm always allocates weight/bias eagerly at init (assumption: it does, since MLXNN LayerNorm normally does; flagging only because the `!` is unconditional). Reachability: model-load path only.
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/CustomAlbert.swift:19 — pooler weight/bias keys are force-unwrapped from the weights dictionary with no shape validation (unlike the LayerNorm guard in AlbertSelfAttention.swift:38-40); a checkpoint missing bert.pooler.weight/bias crashes at load with no named-key diagnostic — apply the same guard-with-message pattern used for LayerNorm. Reachability: model-load path only.

## Coverage
ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/AlbertSelfAttention.swift — findings: 5
ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/CustomAlbert.swift — findings: 2
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/AdaINResBlock1.swift:87,94 — activation uses `MLX.sin(a * xt).pow(2)` where `a` is a learned scalar alpha; if a checkpoint loads alpha1/alpha2 as zero-valued or near-zero tensors, `1/a1` and `a1*xt` produce inf/NaN that silently propagates through the whole residual stack and the returned waveform — no guard or epsilon exists — consequence: a single bad weight slice poisons every downstream sample with NaNs and the caller has no way to distinguish a corrupted checkpoint from a valid one; smallest safe fix: clamp/validate `alpha1`/`alpha2` away from zero at init (e.g. assert `abs(a) > eps` or add `+ eps` before division) so a corrupt checkpoint fails loudly instead of emitting silent NaN audio.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/AdaINResBlock1.swift:31,33,43,45,57,58,64,65,70,71 — every weight lookup uses `weights[key]!` with force-unwrapping on a `[String: MLXArray]` dictionary — a checkpoint missing one expected key (e.g. `.convs1.2.bias` or `.alpha1.2`) crashes the app at model-load time with an unactionable nil-crash instead of a descriptive error — consequence: users get an opaque crash rather than a "corrupt/incomplete model file" message, and there is no path to fall back or report which key was absent; smallest safe fix: replace `!` with `guard let w = weights[key] else { throw ModelLoadError.missingKey(key) }` (or `fatalError` with the key name) so the missing tensor is identified in the crash/error message.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/AdaINResBlock1.swift:16-18 — `getPadding` computes `Int((kernelSize*dilation - dilation)/2)` with Swift `Int` division; for the default `kernelSize=3, dilation∈{1,3,5}` this yields 1, 4, 7 which matches the reference AdaINResBlock1 padding, but the helper silently truncates for any even-product case (e.g. kernelSize=2, dilation=1 → 0 instead of the symmetric 0.5-pad the conv would need) — consequence: a non-default kernel size would produce asymmetric/incorrect padding with no error; smallest safe fix: document the constraint or assert `((kernelSize*dilation - dilation) % 2 == 0)` at init so misconfiguration fails loudly. (info-level only if default config is the only supported one — keeping it low.)
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/AdaIN1d.swift:19-22 — `h.expandedDimensions(axes: [2])` then `split(parts: 2, axis: 1)` assumes the fc output has exactly 2*styleDim channels arranged as [gamma|beta] on axis 1; if a checkpoint's fc weight was trained with a different split ordering (beta-first) the gamma/beta assignment silently swaps and AdaIN modulation produces wrong-but-finite audio — consequence: a subtly wrong checkpoint passes all shape checks and yields degraded voice quality with no error; smallest safe fix: none needed if checkpoint format is fixed upstream — noting only as an assumption-dependent risk, not asserting a defect. (Omitting from findings to avoid speculation; see coverage.)

Reconsidering the last two: the padding helper and the gamma/beta ordering are speculative (no evidence in the shown code that a bad config or swapped checkpoint is reachable), so per the rules ("Report only what the inlined code exhibits") I should not assert them as findings. The force-unwrap and NaN-divisor findings are exhibited by the actual code. Let me also double-check the force-unwrap reachability: this is model-loading code in an iOS app; a corrupt/missing key in a bundled checkpoint is operator/user-facing (app crash at load). Severity: authenticated-user reach is not really the frame here; it's a local inference app. The blast radius is a crash at startup — I'll keep it medium (silent failure mode, no descriptive error) rather than high, since it's not data loss or cross-tenant.

Also checking AdaIN1d.swift:24-25 — `norm(x)` is InstanceNorm1d with `affine: false`; the `(1 + gamma) * normalized + beta` matches the reference. No defect exhibited.

Final findings list: two findings (NaN alpha divisor; force-unwraps). Coverage: both files reviewed; AdaIN1d.swift clean.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/AdainResBlk1d.swift:140 — residual output averaged with shortcut via `/ sqrt(2.0)` — if `shortcut` and `residual` shapes diverge (e.g. `upsampleType != "none"` path pads at line 120 while `shortcut` at line 97–106 applies `upsample` without the same padding), the element-wise `+` broadcasts silently and the result shape/dtype drifts from the expected block output — inference produces subtly wrong audio tensors with no error — add an explicit shape assertion (or `MLX` shape check) before the `+` so mismatched shapes fail loudly instead of broadcasting.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/AdainResBlk1d.swift:120 — `MLX.padded(x, widths: [IntOrPair([0, 0]), IntOrPair([1, 0]), IntOrPair([0, 0])])` hard-codes asymmetric left-pad of 1 on the time axis after transposed-conv upsampling — if the upstream `ConvWeighted` transposed-conv already emits an odd-length sequence, this extra pad shifts the alignment of the residual branch relative to `shortcut`, producing off-by-one temporal misalignment in every upsampled block — derive the pad from the expected output length (or assert `x.shape[1]` matches the shortcut branch length) rather than a fixed `[1, 0]`.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/AdainResBlk1d.swift:115–119 — `pool` is downcast to `Identity`/`ConvWeighted` with `if let`, and if `upsampleType != "none"` but `pool` is neither type, the block silently skips upsampling (x passes through unchanged) — a mis-keyed `upsample` string or wrong `pool` module type yields a silently wrong (non-upsampled) residual instead of a fatal error — replace the silent skip with a `fatalError`/`preconditionFailure` for the unexpected `pool` type.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/AdainResBlk1d.swift:41–43, 55–57, 62–64, 73–74, 80–81, 86–88 — weight lookups use `weights[...]!` force-unwraps on dictionary keys — a missing/misnamed checkpoint key (e.g. `.pool.bias` absent for a layer that has no bias) crashes the app at model-load time rather than surfacing a descriptive error — use `guard let ... else fatalError("missing weight key \(key)")` or a descriptive lookup helper so the failure names the missing key.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/AdaLayerNorm.swift:20–23 — `reshaped([h.shape[0], h.shape[1], 1])` then `split(parts: 2, axis: 1)` assumes `h.shape[1]` is even (gamma/beta halves); an odd `styleDim`-derived width silently produces unequal gamma/beta or a runtime shape error deep in MLX — assert `h.shape[1] % 2 == 0` before splitting to fail at the boundary with a clear message.
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/AdaLayerNorm.swift:26–29 — variance is computed as `MLX.mean(centered * centered, ...)` rather than `E[(x-µ)²]` via a dedicated variance op — this is intentional per the comment (avoids re-reducing), but note the numerical result equals biased variance (divide by N, not N-1), which matches InstanceNorm semantics; no action needed, recorded for traceability.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/ConvWeighted.swift:143,172 — transposed() with no axis arguments defaults to swapping all dimensions, so for a 3-D weight [O,I,K] it produces [K,I,O] rather than the intended [I,O,K] swap of the first two dims — consequence: any conv layer whose input channel count differs from the weight's last dim (the else branch) computes with a mis-shaped weight, throwing a shape error or silently producing wrong logits at synthesis time; smallest safe fix: call weight.transposed(0, 1) (or swapaxes(0,1)) at both sites.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/ConvWeighted.swift:32 — reshapedBias caches `bias!.reshaped([1, 1, -1])` under a cache key that ignores the bias's actual shape, and the reshape is applied to the raw bias rather than the norm-scaled output — consequence: a bias whose element count differs from the conv output channels broadcasts incorrectly (or crashes) and the add at 138/167 applies an unscaled bias to the normalized-weight result, shifting every output frame; smallest safe fix: reshape to [1, 1, 1, -1] (or match conv output rank) and verify the cached shape matches the weight's output-channel count before reuse.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/ConvWeighted.swift:118 — weightNorm divides by (normV + 1e-7) with no guard for a zero-norm weight vector — consequence: a dead channel (all-zero weightV slice) yields a huge normalized value that then multiplies weightG, silently corrupting that channel's activations; smallest safe fix: clamp the denominator or skip normalization when normV == 0.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/Conv1dInference.swift:36-42 — callAsFunction adds bias unconditionally when present but never applies the padding/stride/dilation consistency check, and the bias add uses elementwise `+` without reshaping to broadcast across batch/time — consequence: a bias of shape [C] broadcasts against [B,T,C] correctly in MLX, but a bias of any other rank silently mis-broadcasts; smallest safe fix: reshape bias to [1,1,1,-1] before the add, mirroring ConvWeighted's intent.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Generator.swift:193-194 — `MLX.exp(newX[0..., 0 ..< (postNFFt / 2 + 1), 0...])` and `MLX.sin(...)` assume `newX`'s axis-1 size is exactly `postNFFt + 2`; if `convPost`'s output channel count differs (e.g. checkpoint mismatch), the slice silently produces a spec/phase pair of wrong sizes and `stft.inverse` at line 196 yields garbage audio with no error — assert `newX.shape[1] == postNFFt + 2` before slicing — reachability: any inference call with a mismatched checkpoint, silent garbage output.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Generator.swift:196-197 — `stft.inverse(magnitude:phase:)` result is returned directly with no check that its time axis matches the input `x`'s time axis; if the STFT hop/frame arithmetic drifts by one frame (common with `hopLength == filterLength`), the returned audio is silently longer/shorter than the decoded features — assert the inverse STFT output length equals the expected upsampled length (or trim/pad to match) — reachability: any inference call, silent length drift in output audio.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift:29-34 — `AdainResBlk1d` blocks are built with hard-coded dims (`dimIn + 2`, `1024`, `512`) that must match the checkpoint; if the vendored weights use different channel counts, the force-unwrapped weight lookups (`weights["decoder.encode..."]!` inside the helper, not shown) will either crash on nil or silently load mismatched shapes — pass the expected dims through from config rather than hard-coding, or assert loaded weight shapes match the declared dims — reachability: operator/config-only, crash or silent mismatch.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift:36-58 — `F0Conv`, `NConv`, and `asrRes` are built with force-unwrapped dictionary lookups (`weights["decoder.F0_conv.weight_g"]!` etc.); a missing key in the checkpoint manifest crashes at init with no diagnostic — use `guard let ... else` with a descriptive error naming the missing key — reachability: operator/config-only, crash on model load.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift:74-78 — `MLX.swappedAxes(F0Curve.reshaped([F0Curve.shape[0], 1, F0Curve.shape[1]]), 2, 1)` assumes `F0Curve` is 2-D `[batch, T]`; if a caller passes a 3-D tensor, `reshaped` flattens it incorrectly and the subsequent conv operates on wrong axes — assert `F0Curve.ndim == 2` (and likewise for `N` at line 77) before reshaping — reachability: any caller with a 3-D F0/N tensor, silent garbage.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift:80-88 — `MLX.concatenated([asr, F0, NProcessed], axis: 1)` and the later `[x, asrResidual, F0, NProcessed]` assume all four tensors share the same time-axis length; if `asrResidual`'s length differs from `F0`/`NProcessed` (e.g. after the asr conv's stride/padding), concatenation silently broadcasts or fails — assert equal time-axis lengths before each concatenation — reachability: any inference call, silent misalignment.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift:84-95 — `res` starts `true` and is flipped to `false` only when a block reports `upsampleType != "none"`; if no block ever reports a non-"none" type, `res` stays true for all iterations and the residual concatenation at line 88 runs every loop, changing the tensor shape each iteration and likely failing at `block(x:s:)` — validate that at least one decode block has `upsampleType != "none"` at init, or default `res` to the safer `false` — reachability: operator/config-only, crash or silent wrong output.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift:97 — `generator(x, s, F0Curve)` passes the original 2-D `F0Curve` while `Generator.callAsFunction` (Generator.swift:148) expects a 3-D `[batch, 1, T]` tensor; the shape contract between the two files is inconsistent — normalize the F0 tensor's rank at the Decoder/Generator boundary (reshape to `[batch, 1, T]` before calling) — reachability: any inference call, silent garbage or crash depending on which reshape the callee applies.

## Coverage
ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Decoder.swift — findings: 6
ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/Generator.swift — findings: 15
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:36 — `MLX.where(pDiff .> 0, intervalHigh, pDiffMod)` uses `intervalHigh` (a scalar constant) as the "true" branch instead of the unwrapped phase value, so every frame whose phase diff exceeds π/2 is replaced by the constant π rather than the corrected phase — consequence: phase reconstruction in `inverse` (line 236) is wrong for any signal with rapid phase advance, corrupting the ISTFT output silently — smallest safe fix: compute the wrapped/corrected phase per element (e.g. `MLX.where(pDiff .> 0, pDiffMod + intervalHigh, pDiffMod)`) and feed that into the `where`, matching the numpy `unwrap` semantics.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:142 — `MLX.concatenated(Array(repeating: wSquared, count: t / winLen))` truncates the tiling when `t % winLen != 0`, leaving `windowSumArray` shorter than the tail of `outputStride` for the last partial window — consequence: the final samples of the reconstructed audio are divided by a zero-padded (or missing) window sum, producing NaN/inf or amplitude distortion at the end of every clip whose length is not a multiple of `winLen` — smallest safe fix: use `count: (t + winLen - 1) / winLen` (or append a final partial tile) so the tiled window sum covers the full output length.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:150 — `output[.stride(from: i, by: windowModLen), .ellipsis]` strides the *output* by `windowModLen` (= winLen/hopLen) instead of by `hopLen`, so each overlap-add slice skips `winLen/hopLen` frames rather than the intended hop-aligned frames — consequence: with the shipped 800/200 config the stride is 4× the hop, so most overlapping frames are never summed and the reconstructed audio is a sparse, aliased signal; any config where winLen/hopLen ≠ hopLen breaks entirely — smallest safe fix: stride by `hopLen` (`output[.stride(from: i, by: hopLen), .ellipsis]`) so slices align with the overlap-add grid.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:151 — `windowSumArray = totalWsquared[0 ..< outputStride.shape[0]]` slices the tiled window sum by the *strided-output* length, not by the per-slice output length (`t - i*hopLen` rounded up), so the window-sum and output arrays are misaligned in length — consequence: the concatenated zero-padding at lines 156/162 pads to `t` using different base lengths, making the element-wise division at line 175 operate on mismatched arrays (silent shape error or wrong normalization) — smallest safe fix: derive `windowSumArray` from the same slice length used for `outputStride`'s placement (i.e. slice `totalWsquared` to `t - i*hopLen` before padding), keeping both arrays length `t`.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:94 — `numFrames = 1 + (xArray.shape[0] - nFft) / hopLen` uses integer division that can under-count by one frame when the padded length is not an exact multiple of `hopLen` — consequence: the last partial frame is dropped from the STFT, and the ISTFT then reconstructs slightly-shorter audio than the input (silent truncation) — smallest safe fix: compute `numFrames = 1 + (xArray.shape[0] - nFft) / hopLen` with a ceiling, e.g. `(xArray.shape[0] - nFft + hopLen - 1) / hopLen + 1`, or pad the input to a multiple of `hopLen` before striding.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:15 — `hanning` builds the window from `stride(from: Float(1 - length), to: Float(length), by: 2.0)`, which yields `length - 1` elements instead of `length` for the standard symmetric Hanning definition — consequence: the window is one sample short, shifting the STFT/ISTFT normalization by one frame edge and introducing a small systematic amplitude error — smallest safe fix: generate `length` points (e.g. `stride(from: 0, to: Float(length), by: 1)` mapped through the cosine formula) so the window matches `np.hanning(length)`.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/SineGen.swift:47 — `radValues[...] = radValues[...] + randIni` adds Gaussian noise to only the first harmonic slice (`0 ..< radValues.shape[2]` on axis 1) while `randIni` has shape `[batch, dim]` from line 45; if `radValues.shape[2] != randIni.shape[1]` the broadcast either fails or silently misaligns — consequence: phase noise is applied to the wrong harmonic indices or the op throws at runtime for non-default `harmonicNum` configs — smallest safe fix: broadcast `randIni` across the harmonic axis explicitly (e.g. `randIni.expandedDimensions(axis: 1)`) and add it to the full `radValues` slice, or assert shapes match before the in-place add.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/SineGen.swift:67 — `MLXArray(1 ... harmonicNum + 1)` builds the harmonic range from the *count* rather than the harmonic indices, producing harmonics 1..harmonicNum+1 instead of 0..harmonicNum (or 1..harmonicNum depending on the reference implementation) — consequence: the generated sine components are offset by one harmonic, shifting the pitch of every synthesized waveform — smallest safe fix: construct the range to match the reference harmonic indexing (e.g. `0 ... harmonicNum` or `1 ... harmonicNum`) and verify against the vendored Kokoro source.
