- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/ProsodyPredictor.swift:119 — shared LSTM consumes x.transposed(0,2,1) but the docstring at 113 declares x as [batch, seq_len, channels]; if callers pass channel-major [batch, channels, seq_len] the LSTM sees swapped dims and every downstream F0/N curve is silently misaligned — verify caller layout or normalize once at entry — smallest safe fix: assert/transpose explicitly to [batch, seq_len, channels] at line 119 and document the required input layout.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/ProsodyPredictor.swift:123,139 — F0Val and NVal are both derived from x1.transposed(0,2,1) with no re-mask of padding positions; the LSTM output at padded timesteps is nonzero, so AdaIN blocks and projections produce garbage pitch/voicing values at padded frames that callers may read as real prosody — smallest safe fix: apply the padding mask (zero out padded timesteps) after the LSTM before branching, mirroring TextEncoder's masking discipline.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/ProsodyPredictor.swift:45-101 — init force-unwraps every weights dictionary key (e.g. weights["predictor.shared.weight_ih_l0"]!); a missing or misnamed key crashes at model load with no diagnostic naming the absent tensor — smallest safe fix: use a small helper that throws/fatalError(message:) listing the missing key instead of bare `!`.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TextEncoder.swift:35-76 — same force-unwrap pattern on all weights keys (weights["text_encoder.embedding.weight"]! etc.); missing key crashes load without naming the tensor — smallest safe fix: same helper-based lookup with an explicit error message.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TextEncoder.swift:106,115,118 — masking uses MLX.where(maskT, zeros(like: x), x) where maskT is [batch, seq, 1] broadcast against x in [batch, seq, channels]; the where broadcasts the single-channel mask across channels, which is correct only if mask semantics are per-timestep; but line 135 uses `mask` ([batch, 1, seq]) against x already swapped to [batch, channels, seq] at 131 — if x is channel-major there, the final mask zeroes whole channels instead of padded timesteps, silently corrupting the returned features for any batch with padding — smallest safe fix: confirm axis orientation of `mask` vs the swapped x at 131-135 and align the mask expansion axis to the timestep axis before the final where.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TextEncoder.swift:90 — inputLengths parameter is declared unused ("unused but kept for interface compatibility"); if callers rely on it to trim/validate sequence lengths, that validation silently never happens — smallest safe fix: either use it to assert x's seq dimension matches, or remove it from the signature to stop implying length enforcement.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TextEncoder.swift:123 — fatalError("Unsupported layer type") on an unknown layer in the CNN stack turns a configuration mistake into a hard crash at first inference rather than a load-time validation error — smallest safe fix: validate the layer stack in init (after building cnn) so unsupported compositions fail before the forward pass.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TimestampPredictor.swift:33-34 — `left` is assigned `right` (which was just initialized to `2*max(0, dur[0]-3)`) and the original `left = 0` is discarded, so the first token's start timestamp is anchored to the EOS-style padding offset instead of zero — first phoneme gets a nonzero start_ts and the whole timeline is shifted/overlapping — restore the intended `left = 0` initialization (or compute `right` after `left`) so token 0 starts at 0.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TimestampPredictor.swift:48-49 — whitespace branch advances `i` twice (`i += 1` then `i += 1` again at line 50) around reading `dur[i]`, consuming two duration slots per space token and desynchronizing token/duration indices for all subsequent tokens — every later token's start/end timestamps drift and can read past `dur.count` bounds on the last iteration — advance `i` once per whitespace token (drop the second `i += 1` at line 50, or restructure to match the reference loop).
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TimestampPredictor.swift:61 — `dur[i..<j].reduce(0, +)` sums the token's phoneme durations but `j` was derived from `t.phonemes!.count` while `i` was advanced past whitespace, so the slice can be empty or misaligned, yielding tokenDuration 0 or wrong — align `j` with the actual duration index range consumed by this token before slicing.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/TimestampPredictor.swift:60,64 — `end_ts` is written before `start_ts`-style ordering is guaranteed and `t.start_ts`/`t.end_ts` are set from `left`/`right` floats without clamping to the audio length, so timestamps can exceed the clip duration when `dur` values are large — clamp computed timestamps to `[0, totalSamples]` (or the caller's audio length) before assignment.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/WeightLoader.swift:36-104 — `loadWeights` silently drops every weight whose key has none of the prefixes `bert`/`predictor`/`text_encoder`/`decoder` (the loop only writes into `sanitizedWeights` inside those branches), so an unexpected key (e.g. a renamed module or an extra head) vanishes without error and the model silently runs with missing parameters — collect unmatched keys and throw (or log) instead of returning a silently incomplete dictionary.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/WeightLoader.swift:38 — weights are loaded from `modelPath` with no integrity check (no checksum/manifest/hash verification) before the tensors are fed to the inference engine — a corrupted, truncated, or tampered weight file produces a silently mis-loaded model whose outputs are wrong with no failure signal — verify a pinned checksum/manifest of the weight file before returning `sanitizedWeights`.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/TTSEngine/WeightLoader.swift:117-128 — `checkArrayShape` treats a 3D weight as "already correct" whenever `outChannels >= kH && outChannels >= kW && kH == kW`, which also passes for tensors whose real layout is `(kH, kW, outChannels)` with equal kernel dims, misclassifying them as no-transpose-needed — add an explicit orientation check (e.g. require `outChannels` to be the largest dim by a margin, or compare against the expected layer shape) rather than the current heuristic.

- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:166-171 — hydration silently drops samples whose audio file is missing, and the merged profile's status is recomputed from the surviving samples only — a profile that had reached `readyToTrain`/`trained` can silently regress to `needsData` after a restart (or after a user clears the app documents dir), losing accumulated training progress with no signal — reachability: any authenticated user restarting the app against their own data; consequence is silent data loss of training state — smallest safe fix: keep the sample entry (or at least keep the profile's prior status/adapterPath) when the audio file is absent, e.g. filter only when computing `totalAudioSeconds` for readiness, or log a warning when a sample is dropped.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:185-203 — `merge` prefers the live profile's `status`-independent fields but then overwrites `status` via `_computeStatus`, which ignores `failed`/`training` states recorded on disk — a job that was mid-`training` or had `failed` on disk is reclassified as `readyToTrain`/`needsData` purely from sample counts, so a failed training attempt is reported as ready and a completed-but-adapter-missing profile as trained-then-needsData — reachability: any authenticated user restarting their own session; consequence is wrong UI/training decisions — smallest safe fix: preserve the persisted `status` when it is `training`/`failed`/`trained` and only recompute when the persisted status is `needsData`/`readyToTrain`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:189-193 — union by `audioPath` keeps disk samples whose path matches a live sample only when the live sample list contains that path; if the same recording was re-recorded to a *new* path while the old file still exists, both copies are kept and `totalAudioSeconds` double-counts the same audio, inflating `readiness`/`hasEnoughData` — reachability: any authenticated user re-recording a line; consequence is premature `readyToTrain` and a wasted cloud training job — smallest safe fix: dedupe by transcript+duration or by a stable recording id rather than by file path alone.
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:224-230 — the debounce timer fires `_persistProduction` for every dirty production via `unawaited`, but `_dirtyProductions` is cleared before the futures complete; a `clearProduction` racing the timer can delete the directory while `_persistProduction` is still writing, recreating `profiles.json` in a deleted dir (or throwing inside the unawaited future, which is swallowed by the outer `catch` in `_persistProduction` only if it still exists) — reachability: operator/user clearing a production during the 2s debounce window; consequence is a stale profiles.json resurrecting cleared data — smallest safe fix: re-check `_dirtyProductions`/production existence inside `_persistProduction` before writing, or clear dirty entries only after the persist future completes.
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:253-255 — `tmp.rename(path)` on some platforms fails if `path` already exists (Windows `rename` semantics); persist then throws every time after the first successful write, and the outer catch only debugPrints, so persistence silently stops updating — reachability: operator on Windows/desktop builds; consequence is profiles never re-persisted after the first save — smallest safe fix: delete the existing target before rename, or use `rename` with replace semantics / write-then-swap via a temp dir copy.
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:369-371 — `recommendStrategy` returns `perActor` when `actorsReady >= actorProfiles.length && actorProfiles.isNotEmpty`, but `actorsReady` counts profiles with `hasEnoughData` while `actorProfiles.length` includes profiles with zero samples; the condition is only true when *every* profile is ready, which is intended, yet the pooled branch is unreachable when even one actor is short — a production with one rich actor and one sparse actor always gets `perProduction` even though per-actor LoRA is feasible for the rich actor — reachability: any authenticated user; consequence is suboptimal strategy choice, not data loss — smallest safe fix: decide per-actor when `actorsReady > 0` and pooled data is insufficient, or expose per-actor fallback explicitly.
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:389 — `requestActorTraining` returns silently when the profile lacks enough data; callers cannot distinguish "queued" from "not eligible" — reachability: any authenticated user; consequence is a silent no-op with no user feedback — smallest safe fix: return a `Future<bool>`/status so the caller can surface "not enough data".
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:418-420 — after the placeholder "training" block, status is reset to `readyToTrain` even on the success path, so a profile that just entered `training` ends at `readyToTrain` and `_computeStatus`-style invariants (adapter present ⇒ `trained`) are never reached here; combined with `getBestAdapter` returning `adapterPath` regardless of status, an adapter can be served while the profile claims it is only `readyToTrain` — reachability: any authenticated user; consequence is inconsistent status vs. served adapter — smallest safe fix: set `trained` when an adapter path exists, or gate `getBestAdapter` on `status == trained`.
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:131-133 — `_handleCallback` casts `call.arguments as Map` without a null/type guard; a native side sending `null` or a non-Map for `onResult` throws inside the method-call handler, which the framework swallows as an error reply to native — reachability: any authenticated user during a listen session with a misbehaving native plugin; consequence is dropped result callbacks — smallest safe fix: `as Map?` with a null check, matching the defensive casts used for `onAudioInterruption` (line 154).
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:136-137 — on a final result the handler sets `_listening = false` but never resets `_onResult`/`_onDone`; a subsequent `listen` re-assigns them, but if the consumer relies on `isListening` after a final result without calling `stop()`, state says not-listening while native may still be streaming — reachability: any authenticated user; consequence is stale listening state — smallest safe fix: keep `_listening` in sync only via explicit start/stop, or notify native on final result.

- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_service.dart:232-246 — stop() clears onSilence/onLevel and resets carried transcript state but never resets _sttChannel.onLevel/onAudioInterruption wiring, and stop() sets _onResult/_onDone to null while a pending 200ms continuous restart (scheduled by _startAppleSession's onDone path) may still fire — the restart is guarded only by _isListening/gen, so a stale restart that races a fresh listen() can tear down the new native session and eat the first words of the new utterance — smallest safe fix: cancel/track the pending restart timer in stop() (e.g. store the Timer and cancel it, or bump gen before clearing callbacks) so no stale _startAppleSession can run after stop().
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_service.dart:161-167 — listen() auto-init uses _locale (the field set by init()) but init() itself can be re-entered concurrently; if two listen() calls race, _onResult/_onDone/_vocabHints/_carriedTranscript are overwritten by the second caller while the first session's callbacks are still live, silently redirecting results to the wrong caller — smallest safe fix: guard listen() with a session-scoped callback set (store callbacks per _sessionGen) or reject a second listen() while _isListening is true.
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_service.dart:206-210 — Future.delayed restart is not cancellable; if dispose() runs during the 200ms window, _startAppleSession() is invoked on a disposed channel — smallest safe fix: capture the Timer and cancel it in dispose()/stop().
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_service.dart:131-138 — mergeTranscripts joins fragments with a plain space without checking whether the carried fragment already ends with punctuation/whitespace, so a carried "…end." plus partial "Next…" yields "…end. Next…" correctly but a carried fragment ending mid-word after an auto-finalization could double-join tokens; cosmetic only — smallest safe fix: none needed unless callers observe doubled tokens; document the invariant instead.
- [info] lib/data/services/stt_service.dart:36-60 — init() reads SharedPreferences 'screenshot_mode' and returns false to skip native init; the catch (_) {} swallows all storage errors and falls through to real init, which is the safer direction here (fail toward initializing) — no action needed; noted because the empty catch also hides genuine prefs corruption.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart:127-147 — clearProduction() removes per-actor correction maps for the production but _correctionPatterns entries keyed by wrong-word are evicted only when no surviving correction map references them; patterns whose key equals a wrong word still used by ANOTHER production survive correctly, but patterns for dropped words that are also keys in _vocabularies[otherProduction].correctionCache are never evicted, so stale memoized corrections keep applying to the removed production's words via correct() step 2 — smallest safe fix: also purge matching keys from every surviving vocab.correctionCache when dropping a production.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart:308-317 — _correctWithVocabulary memoizes bestMatch into vocab.correctionCache but the cache is cleared wholesale (clear()) once it reaches _correctionCacheLimit, discarding previously memoized results including entries that were correct; worse, the newly computed bestMatch is stored AFTER the clear, so the cache can momentarily hold the limit-1 old entries plus the new one and then grow past the limit on the next call before being cleared again — the bound is soft and the eviction is all-or-nothing, so a long session repeatedly recomputes the same expensive scans — smallest safe fix: evict a bounded number of oldest entries (or use a LinkedHashMap with remove-first-when-full) instead of clear().
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart:234-238 — learnFromAttempt eviction removes map.keys.first and its compiled pattern, but _correctionPatterns.remove(evict) is called on the top-level map while the pattern may have been created under a different production/actor key collision; if two actors share the same wrong word, evicting one actor's entry drops the shared RegExp still used by the other actor's map — smallest safe fix: only remove the compiled pattern when no other _actorCorrections map still contains the evicted wrong-word key (same shape as clearProduction's stillUsed check).
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart:496-504 — _tokenize lowercases and strips non-word chars but does not strip apostrophes (uses _nonWordSpaceRe which keeps ' since \w excludes it in Dart's RegExp? actually Dart \w = [a-zA-Z0-9_], so apostrophe IS stripped) — verify: _nonWordSpaceRe = [^\w\s] removes apostrophes, contradicting the comment at 63-64 that says apostrophes are preserved for contractions ('tis, o'er) — the vocabulary builder therefore never learns apostrophized forms, weakening hint quality — smallest safe fix: use _nonWordSpaceApostropheRe in _tokenize (or confirm intended behavior with tests).

lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart — findings: 5
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:114 — saveMetadata dereferences `supa.currentUser!` without a null guard — if the user signed out between upload start and metadata save (or the uploader became ready via a stale `isSignedIn` snapshot), a null-check failure throws inside the upload pipeline, which the catch at 407 then treats as a failed upload and retries the job — an authenticated user's own upload can be silently re-attempted/abandoned on sign-out — fix: check `supa.currentUser` before calling and surface a typed error instead of `!`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:470 — backoff delay uses `2 << retryCount` where retryCount is clamped 0–4, but the clamp is applied to the shift operand only after `2 <<` — actually `2 << n` for n=4 gives 32s, fine, but the expression `2 << nextRetry.retryCount.clamp(0, 4)` is correct; however the timer callback at 476–479 moves ALL failed jobs to pending and reprocesses without re-checking `_processing`, so a retry timer firing while `_processing` is true (e.g. a connectivity event started processing) re-enters `_processQueue` which early-returns — harmless — but the timer also fires while jobs were just enqueued, resetting the backoff schedule — low impact; report as [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:476 — retry timer callback mutates `_pending`/`_failed` without the `_processing` guard — a timer firing mid-processing can double-add jobs that enqueue() also re-added, producing duplicate uploads of the same recording — fix: guard the callback with `if (!_processing)` or route through a single scheduler.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:249 — `queuedKeys.add(key)` on a Set used as a dedupe check also mutates the set while iterating `restored`, so a later duplicate job for the same line is silently dropped even if its local file exists and the earlier one's file was missing — direction of failure: the OLDER persisted job wins over the newer live one when the live one was already consumed — consequence: a re-recorded take can be dropped from the queue — fix: compare `recordedAt`/`createdAt` explicitly instead of first-come-wins.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:388 — `file.lengthSync()` on the UI/main isolate performs a synchronous disk stat per upload attempt; on large files or slow storage this blocks the event loop during rehearsal — fix: use `file.length()` async or cache the size.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:213 — `file.existsSync()` inside the serialized chain performs synchronous IO on the main isolate at startup restore — same class as above, startup-only — fix: prefer async `exists()` within the chain.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:247 — `File(job.localPath).existsSync()` per restored job is O(n) synchronous stats at startup — with hundreds of queued jobs this stalls first frame — fix: batch async existence checks.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:114 — `matching[i]` is accessed after `matching` was built from a `where` on `_availableSystemVoices`, but the loop bound uses `matching.length` — safe — however `setLocale` logs full voice metadata unredacted including user locale/voice names to the debug log which is persisted to disk (debug_log_service `_appendSync`) — prompts/media logged unredacted class — consequence: device voice inventory written to a persistent log file readable by anyone with device access — fix: log only counts, not full maps.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:119 — same class: `Voice[$i] full data: ${matching[i]}` dumps entire voice maps into the persistent debug log — fix: redact to name/locale only.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:436 — `speak()` logs the user's script text preview (`"$preview"`) into the persistent debug log — the script text is user content (potentially private rehearsal material) written unredacted to disk — fix: log length/chunk count only.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:460 — same class: `System TTS: "$preview"` logs user text again on the fallback path — fix: redact.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:404 — `stripStageDirections` + `expandAbbreviations` mutate the spoken text before system TTS fallback, but the Kokoro path at 439 synthesizes the SAME stripped text while `speak()`'s caller may expect original text; if stripping removes everything (all-directions line), the empty-text branch at 425 fires completion but never speaks — correct by design — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:760 — `chunkDone.timeout(60s)` swallows the timeout with `catch (_)` and continues — a hung playback leaves the loop waiting on the NEXT chunk's `processingStateStream.firstWhere` which has no timeout at 751 — a stuck stream future can hang `speak()` forever with no completion fired — consequence: rehearsal stalls silently — fix: apply the timeout to the `firstWhere` future as well or race it with a generation check.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:751 — `processingStateStream.firstWhere(completed)` has no timeout and no cancellation; if the player never emits `completed` (e.g. file missing after setFilePath succeeded but decode failed), the awaited future never resolves and `_fireCompletion` never runs — consequence: speak() never returns, rehearsal advance blocked — fix: add `.timeout` and a gen-stale bail.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:684 — `_synthesizeChunk` result is awaited without any timeout; a platform-channel call that never replies (native crash in Kokoro plugin) leaves the chunk future pending forever — consequence: speak() hangs, no completion, no fallback to system TTS — fix: wrap with `.timeout(Duration(seconds: 30))` and treat as failure.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:634 — same class: `_channel.invokeMethod('synthesize')` has no timeout — native side hang = indefinite wait — fix: timeout + fallback.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:246 — `invokeMapMethod('getModelStatus')` inside `_initKokoroMlx` is wrapped in try/catch but the inner `invokeMethod('loadModel')` at 254 has no timeout — a hung native load blocks init() indefinitely and `speak()`'s `await init()` at 402 then blocks the rehearsal — fix: timeout on loadModel.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:830 — `prepareKokoro` fires all chunk synthesis futures concurrently with no concurrency limit — for a long monologue split into dozens of chunks, all native synthesis requests are issued at once — consequence: memory spike / native queue saturation on device — fix: limit in-flight synthesis (e.g. sequential or windowed).
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:828 — `Future.wait(futures)` at 837 resolves each future but individual failures are only logged; the returned `futures` list is handed to `speak()` as `precomputedChunks` where a null-resolving future is handled at 686 — but if EVERY chunk fails, `speak` still enters the Kokoro path and returns false only after all awaited — acceptable — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:686 — when a prefetched chunk resolves null, the code re-synthesizes on demand with `urgent: true` — but `urgent` on ONNX cancels stale queued lines; re-synthesizing with urgent can cancel the very chunk futures still pending in `precomputedChunks` for later chunks — consequence: later chunks' futures resolve null and each triggers another urgent re-synthesis, cascading cancellations — fix: re-synthesize with `urgent: false` for prefetch recovery.
- [low] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:846 — `_currentSpeed` is a plain field mutated by `setRate` with no synchronization; concurrent `speak()` calls (e.g. prefetch + live) read it mid-mutation — Dart single-isolate makes this benign — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:425 — empty-text branch fires completion via microtask but does NOT reset `_isSpeaking` before returning; `_fireCompletion` sets it false — fine — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:216 — system TTS completion guard uses `_speakGen == _activeGen`, but `_activeGen` is only updated in `speak()` at 415; `stop()` at 865 increments `_speakGen` without touching `_activeGen`, so after a stop() a stale system completion can still match `_speakGen == _activeGen` if a new speak() hasn't run — consequence: a stale completion fires `_fireCompletion` advancing rehearsal after an explicit stop — fix: reset `_activeGen` in `stop()` or compare against a stop-generation.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:206 — error handler uses the same `_speakGen == _activeGen` guard — same stale-completion hole as above after stop() — fix: same.
- [low] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:891 — `setCompletionHandler(Function handler)` accepts an untyped `Function`; a caller passing a wrong-arity handler fails only at invocation — fix: type it `void Function()`.
- [low] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:936 — `dispose()` disposes `_audioPlayer` but never cancels `_retryTimer`-like state or resets `_initialized`; a subsequent `init()` re-runs and re-listens `playerStateStream` — single-instance service, dispose likely only at teardown — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:62 — `_tempDir()` caches path but `getTemporaryDirectory()` result is memoized via `??=` on the future — fine — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:107 — `getVoices` cast `as List<dynamic>` on a platform channel result can throw if the native side returns a map — guarded by nothing; would crash setLocale — fix: defensive cast. (Assumption: flutter_tts returns List — verified by plugin contract, so no finding.)
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:287 — `Connectivity().onConnectivityChanged.listen` is never cancelled in `stop()`'s path if `start()` was never called — `_connectivitySub?.cancel()` handles null — fine — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:293 — `_restorePersisted().then(...)` fires `_processQueue` without checking `_processing` — guarded inside `_processQueue` itself — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:351 — `_processQueue` early-returns when `_processing` is true, but `enqueue()` at 342 calls `_processQueue()` unconditionally when `!_processing`; if processing is in flight and a new job is enqueued, the new job is only picked up by the microtask re-kick at 462 — which only runs when the loop exits with pending non-empty — correct — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:400 — `!_pending.remove(job)` treats "job not found" as "superseded", but the job could also have been removed by the file-gone branch at 383 in a previous iteration — in that case `superseded` is true and the code skips marking the local recording with the remote URL even though the upload SUCCEEDED — consequence: a successfully uploaded recording never gets its remoteUrl stamped locally (silent data loss of the upload record) — fix: distinguish "removed because superseded" from "removed because dropped", e.g. a `droppedJobs` set.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:408 — same root cause in the catch path: a job dropped for file-gone then failing upload is treated as superseded and silently discarded — same fix.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:419 — `job.retryCount++` mutates the shared job object also referenced by `_failed` list entries — after `_failed.add(job)` and a later `_pending.addAll(_failed)`, retryCount persists — intended — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:477 — `_pending.addAll(_failed); _failed.clear();` can add duplicates if a job is in both lists (enqueue replaces across lists, so no) — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:182 — `_persist()` serializes `_pending`/`_failed` but `SyncJob.toJson` includes `retryCount` while `fromJson` at 73 always resets `retryCount: 0` — persisted retry budget is silently discarded on restore — consequence: a job that failed 4 times pre-crash restarts with full budget, retrying 5 more times (intended per comment at 70–73, so by design) — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:66 — `fromJson` defaults `durationMs` to 0 when the field is missing — a restored job uploads with duration 0 and `saveMetadata` persists durationMs=0 — consequence: wrong duration stamped on the remote record — fix: require durationMs or skip restore.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:68 — `DateTime.tryParse(...) ?? DateTime.now()` fabricates a createdAt for corrupt entries rather than dropping the job — consequence: freshness comparisons use a bogus time — fix: drop the job when createdAt is unparseable.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:69 — `recordedAt` parse failure yields `null` silently falling back to constructor default (createdAt) — acceptable fallback direction — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:193 — `tmp.rename(path)` on some platforms (Windows) throws if target exists; the catch logs and the queue file is left stale — next persist retries — acceptable — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:191 — temp file `$path.tmp` is never cleaned up if rename fails — orphan file accumulates — fix: delete tmp in the catch.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:288 — `results.any(...)` treats any non-none result as connected; connectivity_plus can emit `wifi` while the network is captive-portal-blocked — uploads then fail and burn retry budget — acceptable offline-first behavior — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:363 — retry timer of 30s created when uploader not ready is never cancelled if `stop()` is called — `stop()` cancels `_retryTimer` — fine — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:132 — `SyncQueue.forTesting` sets `_persistToDisk = persistPath != null` — a test passing a path gets disk persistence writing to arbitrary test paths — test-only — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:505 — `reset()` sets `_loaded = true` then `_persist()` writes cleared state — but `_persist` first calls `_loadPersisted()` which early-returns because `_loaded` — correct — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:209 — `_loadPersisted` sets `_loaded = true` BEFORE attempting parse; if the file read throws (permissions), the queue is marked loaded and never retried — consequence: persisted jobs silently lost for this session — fix: set `_loaded` after successful load, or reset on failure.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:216 — corrupt-file branch renames to `.corrupt` but if rename fails the corrupt file REMAINS at the original path; the next `_persist()` from an empty queue overwrites it (the exact loss the comment warns about) — consequence: queued uploads destroyed — fix: if rename fails, abort the persist (throw) rather than proceeding with an empty snapshot.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:228 — `if (data is! Map) return;` — a JSON array or scalar file (corrupt but parseable) is silently ignored and `_loaded` stays true, so subsequent persists overwrite it — same silent-loss class as above — fix: treat non-map as corrupt and set aside.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:230 — `data['pending'] as List? ?? []` — if `pending` is a Map (corrupt), `as List?` throws inside the try at 211 and the whole restore is logged as failed while `_loaded` is already true — jobs lost silently — fix: validate types per-field.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:232 — `Map<String, dynamic>.from(j)` throws if list elements are not maps — same silent-loss path — fix: `whereType<Map>` already filters, but `.from` on a Map with non-string keys still throws — use explicit safe conversion.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:243 — key building `'${j.productionId}/${j.lineId}'` assumes no `/` in ids; a productionId containing `/` collides keys — consequence: two distinct lines deduped as one — fix: use a separator-safe key (e.g. jsonEncode of a list).
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:325 — job id uses `productionId_lineId_timestamp` with `_` separator — same collision class as above but ids are only for identity, not dedupe — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:270 — `pending`/`failed` getters return unmodifiable views — fine — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:169 — `_serializedFileAccess` chains futures but does not propagate errors: if `action()` throws, `_persistChain` becomes a failed future and every subsequent chained action runs `then` on a failed future, skipping the action body — consequence: after one persist failure, ALL later persists/loads silently no-op (jobs never saved again) — fix: `.catchError` on the chain or use `whenComplete`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:171 — same root cause: `_persistChain = next` keeps the failed future as the chain head; the error is swallowed only by the inner try in `_persist`, but `_loadPersisted` failures (e.g. from `_restorePersisted`) are caught internally too — however any future action throwing outside those catches (e.g. `_persistPath` override logic) breaks the chain permanently — fix: reset chain with catchError.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:267 — `flushPersistence` awaits `_persistChain` which may be a failed future — rethrow at call site — test-only — no finding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:340 — `enqueue()` calls `_persist()` BEFORE `_processQueue()` — if the process uploads and succeeds, the job is removed and `_persist()` runs again — fine — no finding.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:321 — `enqueue` removes matching jobs from `_pending`/`_failed` but does not cancel an in-flight upload of the removed job — the in-flight upload's `saveMetadata` will still stamp the OLD take's URL onto the recording (superseded check at 400 only checks `_pending`) — consequence: a re-recorded take can be overwritten with the older take's remote URL in local metadata — fix: track a per-job generation/token and check it before saveMetadata.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:394 — `saveMetadata(job, url)` is called AFTER upload but the superseded check happens at 400 AFTER saveMetadata — i.e. the metadata write is NOT gated on superseded at all: a job replaced mid-upload still gets its (old) remoteUrl stamped — consequence: local recording metadata points at the wrong (older) take's audio — fix: move the superseded check before `saveMetadata`, or pass a token.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:446 — `onUploaded` fires with `uploadedUrl` only when `!superseded`, but `saveMetadata` at 394 already ran unconditionally — inconsistent ordering as above — same fix (one finding, root cause at 394/400 ordering).

- [medium] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:29-33 — `_serialized` chains mutations but never removes completed entries from `_mutationChains`, so the map grows unboundedly per productionId and every later op on the same key waits on a chain that includes all prior ops — consequence: memory leak across long sessions and, if any op's future never completes (e.g. a hung prefs write), all subsequent overrides/genders/locales mutations for that production are silently queued forever — smallest safe fix: after `run` completes, remove the entry when it is still the current chain (`_mutationChains.removeWhere((k,v) => identical(v, run))` style cleanup) or use a per-key `Lock`/`synchronized` helper.
- [medium] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:79-82 — `getOverrides` catch-all returns `{}` on JSON parse failure — consequence: a corrupt/legacy `voice_overrides_<id>` blob makes every subsequent `setOverride`/`removeOverride` (lines 96-98, 107-109) read `{}`, then `_saveOverrides` writes a map missing all prior entries, permanently destroying the user's stored overrides with no error surfaced — smallest safe fix: on parse failure, fail the read (throw or return a sentinel) rather than silently defaulting to empty, or back up the corrupt blob before overwriting.
- [medium] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:242-245 — same silent-empty fallback in `getGenders` (and identically at 277-280 in `getLocales`): corrupt `character_genders_<id>`/`character_locales_<id>` JSON yields `{}`, and `setGender`/`setLocale`/`renameCharacter` then persist the emptied map — consequence: silent data loss of all per-character genders/locales for that production — smallest safe fix: propagate the parse error instead of returning `{}`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:329-335 — `_renameLoaded` moves the old override to `newName` only when `overrides.containsKey(newName)` is false, but the moved entry keeps `voiceId/speed` while the doc comment (317-318) says an existing entry under `newName` must win — consequence: if `newName` already has an override, `movedOverride` is dropped (removed from `oldName` but never re-added), silently deleting the old character's override during a merge — smallest safe fix: only `overrides.remove(oldName)` when the move will actually be applied, or restore the entry when `newName` already exists.
- [low] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:184 — voice pool fallback `femaleVoices.isNotEmpty ? femaleVoices : maleVoices` resolves to the male pool whenever the female pool is empty, including for characters whose gender is `nonGendered`/unspecified — consequence: gender-sensitive voice assignment silently picks opposite-gender voices when the pool is unpopulated (permissive fallback direction) — smallest safe fix: skip assignment or log when the gender-matched pool is empty instead of cross-falling back.
- [low] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:374 — `resolveVoice` hardcodes `'af_heart'` as the final fallback voice ID — consequence: if the preset pools are empty (e.g. a preset with no voices), every character resolves to one fixed voice with no signal that resolution degraded — smallest safe fix: surface the empty-pool case (throw/log) rather than returning a constant.
- [low] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:220 — `_leastUsedVoice` tie-break `a.value <= b.value ? a : b` plus `counts.entries.reduce` returns the *first* least-used voice with no deterministic ordering guarantee beyond pool order; combined with line 205's unconditional use when all voices are neighbor-taken — consequence: assignment is order-dependent and can silently collide neighbors anyway; acceptable as heuristic but the fallback direction is "assign anyway" rather than "leave unassigned" — smallest safe fix: document/accept, or prefer leaving the character unassigned when every pool voice is neighbor-used.
- [info] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:61,100,348 — `debugPrint` logs production IDs, character names and chosen voice IDs unredacted — consequence: per-production casting metadata can leak into console/logcat in release builds where `debugPrint` is only throttled, not suppressed — smallest safe fix: gate these logs behind `kDebugMode` or strip identifiers.
- [low] lib/data/services/vision_ocr_channel.dart:26-28,64-66 — `MissingPluginException` is swallowed and `null` returned — consequence: on a platform where the native OCR plugin isn't registered (or a typo'd channel name), callers cannot distinguish "no text found" from "OCR unavailable", and `ocrPdf`'s `failedPages`/`pageCount` contract is bypassed entirely — smallest safe fix: rethrow or return a typed error result so callers can degrade explicitly.
- [low] lib/data/services/vision_ocr_channel.dart:16-17,41-43 — result fields are read with `as List?`/`as int?` casts with `??` defaults; a malformed native payload (e.g. `pages` not a `List`, `pageCount` a String) throws a `TypeError` that escapes the `try` (the `try` only wraps `invokeMethod`... actually the casts are inside the same `try`, so it becomes an uncaught `CastError`/`TypeError` outside `on MissingPluginException`) — consequence: a bad native response crashes the caller instead of degrading — smallest safe fix: broaden the catch or validate the map shape before casting.

## Coverage
lib/data/services/vision_ocr_channel.dart — findings: 2
lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart — findings: 8
- [medium] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:31-48 — chunk-walk loop can advance `offset` past the buffer end and then read `sublist(offset, offset+4)`/`getUint32(offset+4)` on the next iteration only if the guard `offset + 8 <= bytes.length` holds; the guard itself is correct, but when a chunk's declared `size` is huge (e.g. corrupt/malicious WAV), `body + size` exceeds `bytes.length`, the loop exits silently, and `_parse` returns a `_WavInfo` built from whatever partial state exists — specifically, if a `data` chunk was already seen with a bogus size, `dataStart`/`dataLength` are taken from that bogus header while `byteRate` may come from an earlier `fmt` chunk, so `prepend` then slices `bytes.sublist(info.dataStart, info.dataStart + info.dataLength)` (line 83) with `dataLength` possibly larger than the remaining bytes — consequence: `RangeError` on malformed input, caught by the blanket `catch (_)` at line 98, which silently returns `source` unchanged — the caller then treats a corrupt WAV as valid audio and plays it rather than reporting the failure — smallest safe fix: clamp `dataLength` to `bytes.length - dataStart` inside `_parse` (it already computes `available` at line 52 but only uses it when `dataLength > available`; also clamp when `dataStart + dataLength > bytes.length` before the `sublist` at line 83) and/or validate `size` against remaining bytes before trusting it.
- [medium] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:53-55 — the `dataLength > available` fallback silently substitutes `available` (the whole rest of file) for the declared data size, so a truncated file whose header claims more data than exists is padded/processed as if the full declared length were present — consequence: the prepended silence length computation (`padBytes` at 77-79) and the rewritten chunk-size fields at 92-94 are computed against a length that doesn't match the actual audio, producing a WAV whose data-chunk size field (line 93-94) is written as `dataLength + padBytes` even though only `available` bytes of audio follow — a mis-sized chunk that players may reject or mis-render — smallest safe fix: when the declared size exceeds the available bytes, either bail out (return null / return source) instead of silently clamping, or write the corrected (clamped) length into the output header rather than the unclamped `dataLength + padBytes`.
- [low] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:98-100 — blanket `catch (_) { return source; }` swallows every failure (I/O errors, RangeErrors from bad headers, unsupported formats) with no logging and no distinction between "not a WAV we understand" and "crashed mid-parse" — consequence: a real bug in the parser (e.g. the RangeError above) is invisible in production; the feature silently no-ops — smallest safe fix: catch narrower exception types or at least log the error via the project's debug log service before returning `source`.
- [low] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:104-119 — `_frameSize` re-walks the chunk list with the same unbounded `size`-advance pattern as `_parse` (line 116) and, if no `fmt ` chunk is found before the loop exits, falls back to the hardcoded `2` (line 118) — consequence: for a non-16-bit-stereo file whose `fmt` chunk is skipped due to a corrupt intervening chunk size, the pad-silence is rounded to a wrong frame boundary, shifting sample alignment — smallest safe fix: reuse the `_WavInfo`/parse result (pass the already-located fmt chunk fields) instead of a second independent walk, and fail loudly rather than defaulting to 2.
- [info] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:76-80 — `padBytes -= padBytes % frameSize` can yield `padBytes == 0` (correctly guarded at line 80 returning `source`), but when `silence.inMilliseconds` is small and `byteRate` low, `round()` may produce 0 and the function returns `source` — this is the safe direction (no mangling), noted only because the doc at 59-63 promises "returns [source] unchanged when... anything goes wrong" while the actual failure mode is indistinguishable from success at the call site; consider surfacing a status instead of relying on the caller to diff paths.

- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/AndroidSttPlugin.kt:165 — RMS level mapping uses `(rmsdB - 2f) / 8f` but Android's relative dB scale is roughly -2..10 with speech typically well above 0 dB, so the `* 0.3f` scaling and the 2..10 window are guesses that can push the reported level to the 0.0 floor during normal speech — Dart-side silence endpointing tuned against this scale (per the comment at 251) can then treat live speech as silence and cut the session early — clamp the window to the documented Android range (-2..10 → 0..1) without the extra 0.3 attenuation, or calibrate against the iOS contract in docs/ANDROID_LIVE_MATCHING.md
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/AndroidSttPlugin.kt:369 — `if (n <= 0) continue` in the capture loop spins forever when `AudioRecord.read` returns 0 or an error (e.g. mic muted mid-capture, RECORDSTATE not recording) with no sleep/backoff, burning CPU on the capture thread and never surfacing the failure to Dart — break out or post an `onError` after a bounded number of consecutive failed reads
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/AndroidSttPlugin.kt:397 — `encoder.getInputBuffer(inIndex)!!` non-null assertion inside the capture thread — a codec in a released/errored state (e.g. after `stopRecording` raced a detach) throws NPE on the capture thread, which is swallowed by the generic `catch (e: Exception)` at 415 and only surfaces as a generic "Capture failed" message — use `?: return`/`?: break` and report the specific failure
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/AndroidSttPlugin.kt:438 — `durationMs` is computed from `recordingStartMs` on the main thread before the capture thread is joined, but `recordingStartMs` is only set in `startRecording` (line 317) and never reset on failure paths that clear `recordingPath` (e.g. `releaseRecorder` at 463) — a second `startRecording` that fails after a prior successful one can report a duration spanning the gap between the two sessions — reset `recordingStartMs = 0` wherever `recordingPath` is cleared

- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:139-147 — recognizeText decodes an arbitrary caller-supplied path with no size bound or decode cap — BitmapFactory.decodeFile on a huge image can OOM the app process before inference; consequence: any authenticated caller (the app's own Dart side, but also any plugin consumer) can crash the process with a single oversized file; smallest safe fix: check File length / inSampleSize decode bounds before decodeFile and fail with INVALID_ARGS beyond a cap.
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:172-201 — ocrPdfPage opens an arbitrary caller-supplied path with no existence/extension check — ParcelFileDescriptor.open throws only for missing files; a directory or non-PDF path reaches PdfRenderer and surfaces as PDF_PAGE_FAILED with the raw exception text; consequence: confusing error channel and wasted render work on non-PDF inputs; smallest safe fix: verify File.isFile and extension/size before opening.
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:204-256 — ocrPdf renders every page of an unbounded PDF sequentially with no page-count or page-size cap — a multi-thousand-page PDF keeps the whole pipeline (render + det + rec per page) running for minutes/hours with progress events only; consequence: unbounded inference work per request, battery/thermal and ANR risk on the main-handler posts; smallest safe fix: cap pageCount per request (e.g. first N pages) and reject beyond with INVALID_ARGS.
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:117-131 — NOT_READY path spawns a raw Thread that busy-polls `loading` every 100ms for up to 15s per pending call — each queued call while models load creates its own polling thread; consequence: N concurrent calls during load = N threads spinning 150 wakeups each; smallest safe fix: register a single pending-call queue drained once when `ready` flips true instead of per-call poll threads.
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:122-126 — the wait thread posts `onMethodCall(call, result)` back onto mainHandler but re-enters the same `!ready` branch if load failed — after load failure `loading` is false and `ready` false, so the re-dispatch falls through to result.error("NOT_READY") — correct, but if load is still in flight past the deadline the same call is re-queued with a fresh 15s deadline, silently extending the wait; consequence: a stuck load keeps callers waiting indefinitely with no error; smallest safe fix: after the deadline, return NOT_READY immediately instead of re-entering the wait branch.
- [low] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:139-147,155,164 — each method call spawns a new unbounded Thread with no concurrency limit — repeated recognizeText/ocrPdf calls during a long PDF job stack up parallel inference threads competing for the same ONNX sessions; consequence: memory blowup and session contention under concurrent callers; smallest safe fix: route work through a fixed-size executor (e.g. 1–2 threads) shared by all OCR methods.
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:296,438 — run() is called with a hard-coded 4-D shape [1,3,H,W] but the model's real input shape is never validated — if det/rec ONNX expect a different rank or batch dim, OnnxTensor.createTensor throws inside the use block and run() returns null, silently degrading to emptyList()/null with no diagnostic; consequence: a mismatched model asset yields silent empty OCR results that look like "no text found"; smallest safe fix: log the model's declared input shape at load and fail loudly (or log) on mismatch instead of returning null.
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:491-503 — run() returns null on any inference failure with no logging — session.run exceptions, missing output tensor, or a non-OnnxTensor result[0] all collapse to null; callers (ocrImage line 297, recognize line 439) then return empty results; consequence: inference failures are indistinguishable from "no text detected" — a real failure is reported as clean/verified empty output; smallest safe fix: catch and Log.e the Throwable inside run() before returning null.
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:108-112 — loadModels catches Throwable and only logs; `ready` stays false and every subsequent call returns NOT_READY with no retry or surfaced error — a transient asset-read failure permanently disables the plugin for the process lifetime; consequence: silent CPU/fallback-equivalent: the Dart side falls back to ML Kit with no record that Paddle was intended and failed; smallest safe fix: record the load Throwable and expose it via a status method, or retry load once.
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:101-103 — model assets are loaded from the app's own flutter assets with no integrity check (no checksum/manifest) — a corrupted or tampered det/rec ONNX in the APK is deserialized by ONNX Runtime without verification; consequence: ONNX Runtime parses attacker-influenced protobuf if the APK is repackaged; smallest safe fix: ship a checksum manifest and verify readAsset bytes before createSession.
- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:335-417 — detectBoxes allocates `visited = BooleanArray(mW * mH)` per call with no bound on mW*mH — for a 960×960 det input that is ~921k booleans per page, fine, but for the unclipped render path (renderPage can produce up to 1800×~2550 px → det input still ≤960) it is bounded; however the seed double loop is O(mW·mH) even when prob is all-background; consequence: ~1M-iteration scan per page even for blank pages, measurable battery cost; smallest safe fix: early-exit when no pixel exceeds detThresh (track any-hit during tensor build or a first pass count).

- [medium] android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/tiltastech/lineguide/PaddleOcrPlugin.kt:406-413 — unclip dist is computed from component area but x1/y1 are clamped to mW/mH then multiplied by sx/sy without clamping to origW/origH — `min(mW, maxX + dist + 2) * sx` can exceed origW when sx rounds up, producing Rect right/bottom beyond the bitmap; cropBitmap clamps (lines 420-424) so downstream is safe, but the returned normalized rect (lines 317-322) can report width/height >1.0; consequence: page-viewer highlight rects can overflow the page bounds; smallest safe fix: clamp x1/y1 to origW/origH after scaling.

- [medium] scripts/deploy.sh:23 — install success is detected by grepping the combined stdout/stderr of `xcrun devicectl device install app` for the literal "App installed" — if devicectl changes wording, emits the phrase on stderr for a different subcommand, or the pipe merges a warning line, a failed install is reported as deployed and launched — authenticated operator only, but a false-positive deploy report can mask a stale build on the device — match on the command's exit status (`if xcrun ... 2>&1 | grep -q ...; then` → capture status via `if xcrun ... ; then` plus a separate log check, or use `xcrun devicectl device install app ... && echo`).
- [low] scripts/deploy.sh:24 — launch failure is silenced by `|| true` and `>/dev/null 2>&1`, so a launch failure (e.g. wrong bundle id, device locked) is reported as "✓ deployed + launched" — operator-only, but the script's success message lies about the launch step — drop `|| true` (or check `$?` and report launch failure distinctly).
- [low] scripts/deploy.sh:15 — `flutter build ios --release 2>&1 | tail -4` swallows the build's exit status (pipefail is set, but the pipeline's status is that of `tail`, so a failed build still prints the last 4 lines and continues to the install loop) — operator-only, wasted retries on a missing build — use `flutter build ios --release 2>&1 | tail -4 ; echo ${PIPESTATUS[0]}` or `set -o pipefail` is already set, so instead capture build status explicitly before continuing.
- [low] scripts/deploy.sh:9 — `DEVICE` is read from the environment with a hard-coded default UDID; if the env var is set to an empty string the script silently targets the default device instead of failing — operator-only — use `DEVICE="${CASTCIRCLE_DEVICE:?set CASTCIRCLE_DEVICE or accept default}"` only when overriding is intended, or validate non-empty after expansion.

- [medium] scripts/fetch-ort-java.sh:19 — `curl -sfL` with `-s` hides progress but `-f` makes curl exit non-zero on HTTP error; because the script has `set -euo pipefail`, a failed download aborts before the SHA check — that is correct — but the *pinned-hash* branch at :22-29 only enforces the pin for `V=1.22.0`; any other version (e.g. `1.22.1`) is downloaded, unzipped, copied into `android/app/libs/` and `jniLibs/` with **no checksum verification at all** — an operator fetching a non-pinned version gets native `.so` files into the app with no integrity check — fail closed: refuse to proceed for any version without a pinned hash (require the caller to add the pin first).

- [low] scripts/fetch-ort-java.sh:34 — `cp "$TMP/classes.jar" ...` assumes the AAR contains `classes.jar`; if the layout changes the script aborts mid-way (after the `unzip`) leaving a partially-copied jar/`jniLibs` tree on disk with no cleanup — operator-only, offline tooling — add a `[ -f "$TMP/classes.jar" ]` guard before the copy loop.
- [low] scripts/fetch-ort-java.sh:45 — `patchelf --clear-symbol-version` is invoked without checking its exit status inside a loop; with `set -e` a failure aborts, but a *partial* symbol-strip (some ABIs patched, some not) leaves mixed-version `.so` files that fail dlopen at runtime — operator-only — verify each `patchelf` invocation and abort before copying the next ABI.
- [info] scripts/fetch-ort-java.sh:16 — the pinned SHA-256 literal is in the script; verify it is not committed if the pin is meant to be private (it is a public artifact hash, so this is informational only).

- [medium] scripts/play-changelog.sh:16 — `VERSION_CODE="${VERSION_LINE##*+}"` strips everything up to the last `+`; if pubspec.yaml lacks a `version:` line grep returns empty and VERSION_CODE becomes empty, so OUT becomes `changelogs/.txt` and the script writes a file Play will never read — consequence: release notes silently land at the wrong filename (fastlane ships no notes) — smallest safe fix: assert VERSION_CODE matches `^[0-9]+$` before using it.
- [medium] scripts/play-changelog.sh:30-31 — TOP_CODE is extracted with `grep -m1 -oE '^## [0-9.]+\+[0-9]+'`; if CHANGELOG.md has no matching heading TOP_CODE is empty and the comparison at line 31 fails open only when it also differs from VERSION_CODE — but when VERSION_CODE itself is empty (see line 16 finding) both sides are empty and the stale-changelog guard passes vacuously — consequence: stale notes can ship as this release's notes — smallest safe fix: validate VERSION_CODE non-empty before the comparison.
- [low] scripts/play-changelog.sh:75-76 — the awk truncation counts `length($0)+1` per line but the final line's newline is not accounted for, so the output can reach 497+newlines and still exceed 500 after `mv` — consequence: Play rejects the file at upload time — smallest safe fix: subtract one more from limit or verify with `wc -c` after mv and re-trim.
- [low] scripts/play-changelog.sh:71 — `wc -c` counts bytes, not characters; multi-byte UTF-8 release notes (e.g. emoji) can exceed Play's 500-char limit while CHARS reports ≤500 — consequence: notes rejected by Play despite the guard passing — smallest safe fix: count with `wc -m` (locale-aware) or python len().

- [medium] scripts/play-preflight.sh:117-121 — `bash -n "$sh"` is run on every `scripts/*.sh` match, but the loop variable `sh` is also used as the file name in the error message; if a filename contains a newline or the glob matches nothing, `for sh in scripts/*.sh` yields the literal pattern and `bash -n` reports a syntax error for a nonexistent file — consequence: preflight can report a false "shell syntax error" for a nonexistent file when no `.sh` files exist (glob unmatched), blocking a release on a phantom defect — smallest safe fix: guard the loop with `[ -f "$sh" ] || continue` (or check `shopt -s nullglob`).

- [medium] scripts/play-preflight.sh:142 — `stat -f %z` is BSD/macOS-only; on GNU/Linux `stat` needs `-c %s` (and `-f` means filesystem), so the size computation fails or returns a filesystem-level value — consequence: on Linux CI the script reports a wrong/zero size or exits via `set -e` with an unrelated error, masking the real preflight result — smallest safe fix: use a portable size probe (`wc -c < "$AAB"`) or branch on `stat` flavor.

- [low] scripts/play-preflight.sh:71,89,103 — `sips -g pixelWidth -g pixelHeight` is macOS-only; on Linux the `2>/dev/null` hides the failure and `W`/`Hh` become empty, so the dimension checks compare empty strings — consequence: icon/featureGraphic/screenshot dimension gates silently pass on non-macOS hosts — smallest safe fix: detect `sips` absence and fail the check explicitly (or use a portable image-size probe).

- [medium] scripts/ship-play.sh:104 — `fastlane android "$LANE" $LANE_ARGS` word-splits `LANE_ARGS` (acknowledged by the shellcheck disable); `--validate` builds `validate:true` into it, but a future `--live`+`--validate` combination produces `status:completed validate:true` which fastlane may interpret positionally — consequence: an unintended rollout option reaches the upload tool — smallest safe fix: build an array `LANE_OPTS=(${LANE_ARGS})` and expand as `"${LANE_OPTS[@]}"`, keeping the disable comment only if intentional.
- [low] scripts/ship-play.sh:36–39 — the guard rejects `--live/--validate` without `--closed`, but `--build --live --closed` still passes `status:completed` into a build-only run harmlessly; no defect.
- [medium] scripts/ship-play.sh:81 — `./scripts/play-preflight.sh` is invoked with no timeout and its failure is the only gate before upload; if it hangs, the operator's CI blocks indefinitely — smallest safe fix: `timeout 300 ./scripts/play-preflight.sh` (assumption: preflight is a long-running local script; not visible here).
- [low] scripts/ship-play.sh:92 — the service-account JSON path is checked for existence but never for permissions; the file itself is not read by this script — no finding.
- [info] scripts/ship-play.sh:92 — `~/.google-play/play-store-key.json` is a Play service-account secret on disk; verify it is not committed (script only checks existence; repo state unverifiable here).

## Coverage
scripts/pull-debuglog.sh — findings: 2
scripts/ship-play.sh — findings: 5
- [medium] scripts/ship-testflight.sh:59-61 — pubspec.yaml version bump uses `sed -i '' -E` which is BSD/macOS-only syntax; on Linux (GNU sed) `-i ''` treats `''` as the script and fails or corrupts the file — consequence: the bump step silently fails or rewrites pubspec.yaml wrongly on a Linux CI runner, shipping a stale build number — smallest safe fix: detect sed flavor or use a portable in-place edit (e.g. `sed -i.bak` + mv, or perl).
- [medium] scripts/ship-testflight.sh:64 — WANT is re-derived from pubspec.yaml after the bump, but if `--no-bump` is passed the bump block is skipped and WANT still reads the current version; that is fine, yet if the bump block ran and sed failed under `set -e` the script exits — the real defect: WANT is parsed from the same file the bump just rewrote, so a malformed pubspec (e.g. version line with trailing comment) makes GOT/WANT compare against a wrong literal and the gate at 74 can pass with a stale archive — consequence: a silently-stale archive ships the prior build, exactly the trap the header warns about — smallest safe fix: assert the bumped line matches `^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\+[0-9]+$` before using it, and fail loudly if not.
- [low] scripts/ship-testflight.sh:68 — `rm -rf "$ARCHIVE" build/ios/ipa` deletes prior artifacts before the new build; if `flutter build ios` then fails, the previous good archive/IPA is gone — consequence: an operator loses the last shippable artifact on a failed rebuild — smallest safe fix: build into a temp dir or move old artifacts aside and restore on failure.
- [low] scripts/ship-testflight.sh:94 — `xcrun altool --upload-app` has no retry/timeout and its failure under `set -e` aborts before the dSYM step; the header says dSYMs are best-effort but the upload itself is the ship — consequence: transient App Store Connect errors fail the whole ship run with no retry — smallest safe fix: add a bounded retry loop around the upload.
- [info] scripts/ship-testflight.sh:39-49 — ASC API key id/issuer are read from env or a git-ignored ids.env; the .p8 itself lives outside the repo — verify it is not committed (no secret literal appears in this file; only identifiers, which is acceptable).
- [medium] scripts/verify-apk-ort.sh:12 — `find ~/.pub-cache/hosted/pub.dev -path "*sherpa_onnx_android_arm64*/jniLibs/arm64-v8a/libonnxruntime.so" | head -1` picks the FIRST matching version in the cache, not the version the project pins — consequence: with multiple sherpa_onnx versions cached (common after a dependency bump), the script compares the APK against the wrong .so and can pass (or fail) spuriously, reporting "OK" for a mismatched C-runtime — smallest safe fix: resolve the exact version from the lockfile (e.g. grep pubspec.lock / .package or `flutter pub deps`) and match that path, or fail if more than one match exists.
- [low] scripts/verify-apk-ort.sh:15 — `unzip -p "$APK" ... | strings | grep ... | head -1` under `set -o pipefail`: `head -1` closing the pipe early can SIGPIPE `strings`/`unzip`, making GOT empty and the comparison fail (or, if GOT is empty and WANT non-empty, fail loudly — safe direction) — consequence: spurious FAIL on large APKs in some environments; note the script itself documents this exact trap for the later grep at 20-21 but not here — smallest safe fix: use `grep -m1` instead of `head -1`, or drop pipefail for these pipelines.
- [low] scripts/verify-apk-ort.sh:22-23 — `unzip -l "$APK" | grep "lib/..." > /dev/null` under pipefail: grep without `-q` still exits 0 on match, but if the entry is absent grep exits 1 and unzip may SIGPIPE — the documented trap applies here too; the `||` branch handles the failure but conflates "missing entry" with "pipeline error" — consequence: acceptable (fails safe), but the error message may mislead — smallest safe fix: use `grep -q` with `set +o pipefail` around it, or capture unzip output first.

- [medium] ios/Runner/MisakiVendored/English/Num2Word/EnglishNum2Word.swift:223-224 — fractional digits are derived by string-formatting a Decimal (`"\(fractionalPart)"` then `dropFirst(2)`), which depends on locale/Decimal description and silently maps unparseable digits to 0 — e.g. a Decimal whose description is "0.5" works, but one printed as "0.05" yields "5"→"five" instead of "zero five", and any non-digit rendering yields "zero" per digit — consequence: wrong spoken decimal words for legitimate inputs, silent data corruption in TTS output — smallest safe fix: derive fractional digits from the Decimal's numeric value (e.g. scale/exponent API) instead of string manipulation, and skip non-digit characters rather than defaulting to 0.
- [medium] ios/Runner/MisakiVendored/English/Num2Word/EnglishNum2Word.swift:145-156 — `toCardinal` handles thousands via `midNumWords` before consulting `cardsDescending`, so values ≥ 1000 are matched by the first entry (1000, "thousand") and the "illion" table is unreachable for any number ≥ 1000 — consequence: 1,000,000 prints as "one thousand thousand" (quotient 1000 → "one thousand", word "thousand"), and larger powers print as repeated "thousand" segments instead of "million"/"billion" — smallest safe fix: remove the 1000 entry from `midNumWords` (or route numbers ≥ 1000 to the `cardsDescending` loop) so the high-word table handles them.
- [low] ios/Runner/MisakiVendored/English/Num2Word/EnglishNum2Word.swift:190 — year branch condition `(high % 10 == 0 && low < 10)` misclassifies years like 2005 (high=20, low=5 → cardinal "two thousand five" is intended, but condition sends it to cardinal anyway, which is fine) while 1905 (high=19, low=5) goes to the "nineteen oh-five" branch correctly; however 2000–2009 with low<10 and high%10==0 is handled, and 2100 (high=21, low=0) prints "twenty-one hundred" instead of "twenty-one hundred" — consequence: minor phrasing inconsistency for round centuries (e.g. 2100 → "twenty-one hundred" vs expected "two thousand one hundred") — smallest safe fix: none required if "hundred" phrasing is acceptable; otherwise add a low==0 && high%10!=0 special case. (Reported as low because output is still valid English.)
- [low] ios/Runner/MisakiVendored/English/Num2Word/EnglishNum2Word.swift:99-108 — `toOrdinalNum` returns "" for any input whose ordinal rendering is shorter than 2 characters (e.g. number 1 → ordinal "first" is fine, but a zero/negative input yields empty string) — consequence: callers get an empty string rather than a numeric ordinal like "1st" for edge inputs — smallest safe fix: fall back to `"\(number)th"`-style numeric suffix when the ordinal path yields an empty string.
- [low] ios/Runner/MisakiVendored/Extensions/MLXArray+DebugPrint.swift:27 — `x.asType(Float.self)` silently reinterprets every dtype (including bfloat16/half/int/bool) as Float32 — consequence: debug output for integer/bool arrays shows float-formatted values (e.g. "1.000e+00") rather than raw values, misleading debugging — smallest safe fix: branch on dtype and use the matching `asArray` for integer/bool types, or document the coercion.
- [low] ios/Runner/MisakiVendored/Extensions/MLXArray+DebugPrint.swift:44 — `rows = max(1, flat.count / cols)` can mis-handle ragged N-D shapes when the last dimension doesn't evenly divide the flattened count (impossible for valid MLX shapes, but if `cols` is 0 the guard at :43 already returns) — consequence: none for valid arrays; only a defensive note — smallest safe fix: none required.

- [medium] macos/Runner/VisionOcrPlugin.swift:44 — `args["scale"]` is read from the raw `Any` dictionary but the guard only validated `path`; a non-Double scale silently falls back to 2.0 while a Double outside [0.5, 4.0] is clamped — the clamp direction is safe, but the fallback resolves to the riskier default (2.0) when the caller sends a malformed value, meaning a caller that intended scale=0.5 gets a 4x larger bitmap allocation than requested — smallest safe fix: fail the call with `INVALID_ARGS` when `args["scale"]` is present but not a Double, instead of `?? 2.0`.
- [high] macos/Runner/VisionOcrPlugin.swift:100-101 — page render dimensions are `bounds.width * scale` with no upper bound on the PDF's own media-box size; a hostile PDF with a huge media box (e.g. 100000×100000 pt) at scale 4.0 produces a multi-GB CGContext allocation per page and jetsams the process mid-OCR — smallest safe fix: clamp the final rendered width/height to a sane maximum (e.g. 4096 px) after scaling, before allocating the context.
- [medium] macos/Runner/VisionOcrPlugin.swift:93-119 — the whole PDF is OCR'd page-by-page inside one `DispatchQueue.global` block with no concurrency limit and no per-page timeout; a large PDF blocks the shared global pool for minutes and a hung `handler.perform` (Vision can stall on pathological images) has no cancellation path — smallest safe fix: run recognition with a `VNImageRequestHandler` on a dedicated serial queue or add a watchdog/timeout around `perform`.
